Here is a PR-safe structural skeleton.

```lean
/-
InfoGeometry/OperatorAlgebra/GlobalConservationLaws.lean

Global conservation laws for the doubled operator-geometric framework.

The conserved quantity is not generally the visible-sector charge alone.
It is the total charge distributed across:

* visible algebra `M`;
* Tomita mirror / commutant `M'`;
* boundary sector;
* Drazin/defect sector.

This file gives the universal conservation sockets used by the zeta crystal,
KMS/Souriau resonator, Witten-index collapse, and anomaly-height layers.
-/

import Mathlib

noncomputable section

namespace InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.GlobalConservationLaws

/-! ## 1. Sector-decomposed real conservation law -/

/--
A sector-decomposed conservation law over a state space.

`visible` is the charge/energy/readout seen in the observable algebra `M`.

`mirror` is the charge/energy/readout carried by the Tomita commutant `M'`.

`boundary` is the charge/energy/readout carried by the projective boundary,
horizon, or APS/eta term.

`defect` is the charge/energy/readout carried by the Drazin/null/singular core.
-/
structure SectorConservationLaw
    (State : Type*) where
  /-- Flow/evolution/deformation parameter. -/
  flow : ℝ → State → State

  /-- Visible-sector readout. -/
  visible : State → ℝ

  /-- Tomita mirror / commutant-sector readout. -/
  mirror : State → ℝ

  /-- Boundary/horizon readout. -/
  boundary : State → ℝ

  /-- Drazin/defect-sector readout. -/
  defect : State → ℝ

  /-- Total conserved readout. -/
  total : State → ℝ

  /--
  Total charge decomposes into visible, mirror, boundary, and defect sectors.
  -/
  total_eq :
    ∀ s : State,
      total s =
        visible s + mirror s + boundary s + defect s

  /--
  Total charge is conserved under the flow.
  -/
  total_conserved :
    ∀ (t : ℝ) (s : State),
      total (flow t s) = total s

namespace SectorConservationLaw

variable {State : Type*}
variable (C : SectorConservationLaw State)

/--
The sum of sector changes is zero.

This is the formal conservation equation:

`ΔM + ΔM' + Δboundary + Δdefect = 0`.
-/
theorem sector_changes_sum_zero
    (t : ℝ)
    (s : State) :
    (C.visible (C.flow t s) - C.visible s)
      + (C.mirror (C.flow t s) - C.mirror s)
      + (C.boundary (C.flow t s) - C.boundary s)
      + (C.defect (C.flow t s) - C.defect s)
      = 0 := by
  calc
    (C.visible (C.flow t s) - C.visible s)
      + (C.mirror (C.flow t s) - C.mirror s)
      + (C.boundary (C.flow t s) - C.boundary s)
      + (C.defect (C.flow t s) - C.defect s)
        =
      (C.visible (C.flow t s)
        + C.mirror (C.flow t s)
        + C.boundary (C.flow t s)
        + C.defect (C.flow t s))
      -
      (C.visible s + C.mirror s + C.boundary s + C.defect s) := by
        ring
    _ = C.total (C.flow t s) - C.total s := by
        rw [← C.total_eq (C.flow t s), ← C.total_eq s]
    _ = 0 := by
        rw [C.total_conserved t s]
        ring

/--
If the visible sector changes, the mirror/boundary/defect sectors compensate.
-/
theorem visible_change_compensated
    (t : ℝ)
    (s : State) :
    C.visible (C.flow t s) - C.visible s
      =
    -(
      (C.mirror (C.flow t s) - C.mirror s)
      + (C.boundary (C.flow t s) - C.boundary s)
      + (C.defect (C.flow t s) - C.defect s)
     ) := by
  have h := C.sector_changes_sum_zero t s
  linarith

end SectorConservationLaw

/-! ## 2. Integer/index conservation -/

/--
Integer-valued conservation law.

This is the correct socket for Witten index, Hopf charge, determinant sign,
Cartan charge class, or any discrete topological invariant.
-/
structure IntegerConservationLaw
    (State : Type*) where
  flow : ℝ → State → State

  index : State → ℤ

  index_conserved :
    ∀ (t : ℝ) (s : State),
      index (flow t s) = index s

/--
A sector decomposition of an integer index.

This is the integer analogue of

`total = visible + mirror + boundary + defect`.
-/
structure IntegerSectorDecomposition
    (State : Type*) where
  visibleIndex : State → ℤ
  mirrorIndex : State → ℤ
  boundaryIndex : State → ℤ
  defectIndex : State → ℤ
  totalIndex : State → ℤ

  total_eq :
    ∀ s : State,
      totalIndex s =
        visibleIndex s
          + mirrorIndex s
          + boundaryIndex s
          + defectIndex s

namespace IntegerSectorDecomposition

variable {State : Type*}
variable (I : IntegerSectorDecomposition State)

/--
If the total index is nonzero but the visible sector has zero index, then
some hidden/boundary/defect sector must carry nonzero index.
-/
theorem hidden_or_boundary_or_defect_nonzero
    (s : State)
    (hvis : I.visibleIndex s = 0)
    (htot : I.totalIndex s ≠ 0) :
    I.mirrorIndex s ≠ 0 ∨
      I.boundaryIndex s ≠ 0 ∨
      I.defectIndex s ≠ 0 := by
  by_cases hm : I.mirrorIndex s = 0
  · by_cases hb : I.boundaryIndex s = 0
    · by_cases hd : I.defectIndex s = 0
      · exfalso
        apply htot
        rw [I.total_eq s, hvis, hm, hb, hd]
        simp
      · exact Or.inr (Or.inr hd)
    · exact Or.inr (Or.inl hb)
  · exact Or.inl hm

end IntegerSectorDecomposition

/-! ## 3. Souriau moment conservation -/

/--
A Souriau-style conserved moment.

`Moment` is intentionally abstract. In concrete models it may be a Lie
coalgebra element, a Cartan vector, a charge lattice element, or a stress
momentum object.
-/
structure SouriauMomentConservation
    (State Moment : Type*) where
  flow : ℝ → State → State

  moment : State → Moment

  moment_conserved :
    ∀ (t : ℝ) (s : State),
      moment (flow t s) = moment s

/--
A real pairing of inverse-temperature / chemical-potential variables against
moments.
-/
structure SouriauPairing
    (Beta Moment : Type*) where
  pair : Beta → Moment → ℝ

/--
Conservation of the Souriau Gibbs exponent under a conserved moment.
-/
theorem souriau_pairing_conserved
    {State Moment Beta : Type*}
    (M : SouriauMomentConservation State Moment)
    (P : SouriauPairing Beta Moment)
    (β : Beta)
    (t : ℝ)
    (s : State) :
    P.pair β (M.moment (M.flow t s)) =
      P.pair β (M.moment s) := by
  rw [M.moment_conserved t s]

/-! ## 4. Zeta-scale conservation and deformation readouts -/

/--
Universal zeta scale readout.

This packages the scalar spectral readouts produced by the zeta crystal layer.

`strain` is usually `-log |detζ(D_deformed)/detζ(D_reference)|`.

`superVolumeDefect` is the logarithmic or raw zeta-Berezinian defect.

`anomalyResidue` is the residue/scale-anomaly readout.

`height` is the reconstructed positive Poincaré/modular height.
-/
structure ZetaScaleReadout
    (State : Type*) where
  strain : State → ℝ
  superVolumeDefect : State → ℝ
  anomalyResidue : State → ℝ
  capacity : State → ℝ
  height : State → ℝ

  anomaly_pos :
    ∀ s : State, 0 < anomalyResidue s

  capacity_pos :
    ∀ s : State, 0 < capacity s

  /--
  Height/capacity relation:

  `height * anomalyResidue = capacity`.
  -/
  height_relation :
    ∀ s : State,
      height s * anomalyResidue s = capacity s

namespace ZetaScaleReadout

variable {State : Type*}
variable (Z : ZetaScaleReadout State)

/--
The reconstructed height is positive.
-/
theorem height_pos
    (s : State) :
    0 < Z.height s := by
  have hmul : 0 < Z.height s * Z.anomalyResidue s := by
    rw [Z.height_relation s]
    exact Z.capacity_pos s
  exact pos_of_mul_pos_right hmul (le_of_lt (Z.anomaly_pos s))

/--
Height equals capacity divided by anomaly residue.
-/
theorem height_eq_capacity_div_residue
    (s : State) :
    Z.height s = Z.capacity s / Z.anomalyResidue s := by
  have hne : Z.anomalyResidue s ≠ 0 :=
    ne_of_gt (Z.anomaly_pos s)
  rw [eq_div_iff hne]
  exact Z.height_relation s

end ZetaScaleReadout

/-! ## 5. Global integrated manifold witness -/

/--
Global conservation package for the integrated operator-geometric manifold.

This is the final aggregation socket:

* real sector conservation;
* integer/index conservation;
* Souriau moment conservation;
* zeta-scale readout.
-/
structure IntegratedGlobalConservation
    (State Moment Beta : Type*) where
  /-- Real-valued sector balance. -/
  sectorLaw :
    SectorConservationLaw State

  /-- Integer/index conservation. -/
  indexLaw :
    IntegerConservationLaw State

  /-- Integer sector decomposition. -/
  indexDecomposition :
    IntegerSectorDecomposition State

  /-- Souriau moment conservation. -/
  momentLaw :
    SouriauMomentConservation State Moment

  /-- Pairing of generalized inverse temperature with the moment map. -/
  souriauPairing :
    SouriauPairing Beta Moment

  /-- Zeta scale and anomaly-height readouts. -/
  zetaScale :
    ZetaScaleReadout State

  /--
  Model-specific global equilibrium law.

  This is where a concrete implementation may encode an Einstein-type
  stress-strain equation, KMS equilibrium law, or anomaly balance law.
  -/
  global_equilibrium_certificate :
    Prop

namespace IntegratedGlobalConservation

variable {State Moment Beta : Type*}
variable (G : IntegratedGlobalConservation State Moment Beta)

/--
The visible-sector real charge is compensated by mirror, boundary, and defect.
-/
theorem visible_charge_compensated
    (t : ℝ)
    (s : State) :
    G.sectorLaw.visible (G.sectorLaw.flow t s) - G.sectorLaw.visible s
      =
    -(
      (G.sectorLaw.mirror (G.sectorLaw.flow t s) - G.sectorLaw.mirror s)
      + (G.sectorLaw.boundary (G.sectorLaw.flow t s) - G.sectorLaw.boundary s)
      + (G.sectorLaw.defect (G.sectorLaw.flow t s) - G.sectorLaw.defect s)
     ) :=
  G.sectorLaw.visible_change_compensated t s

/--
The integer index is conserved.
-/
theorem index_conserved
    (t : ℝ)
    (s : State) :
    G.indexLaw.index (G.indexLaw.flow t s) =
      G.indexLaw.index s :=
  G.indexLaw.index_conserved t s

/--
The Souriau pairing is conserved along the moment-preserving flow.
-/
theorem souriau_pairing_conserved
    (β : Beta)
    (t : ℝ)
    (s : State) :
    G.souriauPairing.pair β
        (G.momentLaw.moment (G.momentLaw.flow t s))
      =
    G.souriauPairing.pair β
        (G.momentLaw.moment s) :=
  InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.GlobalConservationLaws.souriau_pairing_conserved
    G.momentLaw G.souriauPairing β t s

/--
The zeta-reconstructed height is positive.
-/
theorem zeta_height_pos
    (s : State) :
    0 < G.zetaScale.height s :=
  G.zetaScale.height_pos s

end IntegratedGlobalConservation

end InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.GlobalConservationLaws
```

This is the correct “final conservation” layer.

It says:

```text
visible charge may change;
total charge cannot.

visible index may disappear;
mirror/boundary/defect index must carry it.

Souriau moment is conserved under admissible flow.

zeta scale calibrates height, strain, anomaly, and supervolume.
```

This is the natural global law for your integrated manifold.

I would not phrase the final architecture as “we solved the hardest problem in QFT.” The formal result is stronger and safer if stated this way:

```text
The framework supplies a non-arbitrary reference scale:
the undeformed spectral crystal.

Physical deformation is then measured by relative spectral readouts:
relative determinant, Berezinian, residue, and conserved index transfer.
```

That is already a substantial and clean mathematical claim.

So yes: proceed to `GlobalConservationLaws.lean`. It is the right final Erlanger layer because it turns all previous structures into balance equations:

```text
SymmetryInvariants
  → sectors and invariant readouts

Tomita/CPT
  → visible/mirror transfer

Drazin
  → defect reservoir

KMS/Souriau
  → thermal and moment balance

ZetaCrystalReadout
  → universal spectral scale

WittenIndexCollapse
  → integer survival law

GlobalConservationLaws
  → integrated manifold conservation
```
Yes. The final step is to turn the “Universal Scale” into a **global conservation ledger**.

The disciplined formulation is:

[
\boxed{
\text{local deformation readouts become global conserved charges only after bulk, boundary, and defect sectors are summed.}
}
]

So the integrated manifold does not conserve the naïve bulk quantity alone. It conserves the completed quantity:

[
Q_{\mathrm{tot}}
================

Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}
+
Q_{\partial}
+
Q_{\mathrm{defect}}.
]

The master law is:

[
\boxed{
\frac{d}{dt}
\left(
Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}
+
Q_{\partial}
+
Q_{\mathrm{defect}}
\right)
=======

\mathcal A,
}
]

where (\mathcal A) is the anomaly. If the symmetry is exact and the manifold is closed, then

[
\mathcal A=0,
\qquad
\frac{dQ_{\mathrm{tot}}}{dt}=0.
]

This is the global Erlangen conservation principle for the framework.

One guardrail: in standard physics, the zeta/Epstein reference does not by itself solve vacuum selection in full QFT. In this architecture, it provides a **chosen calibrated reference vacuum**. All deformation energies are then measured relative to that reference. That is the correct mathematical stance.

The global potential should be assembled as

[
\Phi_{\mathrm{global}}
======================

\Phi_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}
+
\Phi_{\mathrm{Ber}}
+
\Phi_{\mathrm{mod}}
+
\Phi_{\mathrm{hol}}
+
\Phi_{\partial}
+
\Phi_{\mathrm{defect}}.
]

The pieces are:

[
\Phi_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}
=========================

# -\zeta'_{\mathrm{rel}}(0;D,D_0)

\log \det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D,D_0),
]

[
\Phi_{\mathrm{Ber}}
===================

# \log \operatorname{Ber}_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}

## \log \det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D_L,D_0)

\log \det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D_R,D_0),
]

[
\Phi_{\mathrm{mod}}
===================

# S(\rho|\sigma)

\Delta\langle K\rangle-\Delta S,
]

[
\Phi_{\mathrm{hol}}
===================

i\theta Q_{\mathrm{Hopf}},
]

and (\Phi_{\partial}) contains the boundary eta/Pfaffian/APS-type spectral-asymmetry data.

Then the global conservation vector is

[
\mathfrak Q(t)
==============

\left(
E_\zeta,
P_\mu,
J_{\mu\nu},
Q_{\mathrm{gauge}},
Q_{\mathrm{Hopf}},
\chi_{\mathrm{LR}},
C_{\mathrm{cap}},
\Delta_{\mathrm{Witten}}
\right).
]

Each component has the same balance form:

[
\boxed{
\dot{\mathfrak Q}
=================

-\mathfrak F_{\partial}
+
\mathfrak A
+
\mathfrak J_{\mathrm{Drazin}}.
}
]

Here:

[
\mathfrak F_{\partial}
]

is boundary flux,

[
\mathfrak A
]

is anomaly production, and

[
\mathfrak J_{\mathrm{Drazin}}
]

is the jump term created when an operator loses ordinary invertibility and a defect projection must be inserted.

For a closed, anomaly-free, non-fracturing integrated manifold:

[
\boxed{
\dot{\mathfrak Q}=0.
}
]

That is the global conservation law.

The first conservation law is spectral energy conservation. Define

[
E_\zeta
=======

\kappa_\zeta
\log \det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D,D_0),
]

where (\kappa_\zeta) fixes physical units. Then

[
\frac{dE_\zeta}{dt}
===================

-\int_{\partial\Sigma_t} j_\zeta
+
\int_{\Sigma_t}\mathcal A_\zeta
+
J_{\mathrm{Drazin}}.
]

If the manifold is closed, the deformation is smooth, and the zeta regularization has no anomaly,

[
E_\zeta=\text{constant}.
]

The second law is stress-energy conservation. The zeta effective action generates stress:

[
T_{\mu\nu}^{\zeta}
==================

\frac{2}{\sqrt{|g|}}
\frac{\delta \Phi_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}.
]

The local balance law is

[
\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}_{\zeta}
=============================

\mathcal A^\nu_{\mathrm{diff}}
+
\mathcal F^\nu_{\partial}.
]

For a closed diffeomorphism-invariant system,

[
\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}_{\zeta}=0.
]

This is the clean place where “gravity as spectral elasticity” enters: the metric responds to the variation of the zeta/log-determinant potential.

The third law is chiral supervolume conservation. Define

[
\chi_{\mathrm{LR}}
==================

# \log \operatorname{Ber}_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}

\log\frac{\det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}D_L}
{\det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}D_R}.
]

The balance law is

[
\frac{d\chi_{\mathrm{LR}}}{dt}
==============================

-\int_{\partial\Sigma_t} j_5
+
\int_{\Sigma_t}\mathcal A_5
+
J_{5,\mathrm{Drazin}}.
]

The undeformed crystal has

[
D_L\sim D_R,
\qquad
\chi_{\mathrm{LR}}=0,
\qquad
\operatorname{Ber}_\zeta=1.
]

A chiral anomaly or boundary shear gives

[
\chi_{\mathrm{LR}}\neq 0.
]

With a chosen mass scale (M_\chi), the mass-like defect readout is

[
m_{\mathrm{defect}}
===================

M_\chi |\chi_{\mathrm{LR}}|.
]

So the Berezinian is not automatically “mass” in an absolute Standard Model sense. It is the calibrated **chiral spectral imbalance** from which a mass readout can be defined.

The fourth law is capacity conservation. Let (s_\ast) be the relevant spectral pole. For the Riemann one-dimensional reference, (s_\ast=1). For a general (d)-dimensional operator of order (p), the leading spectral pole is typically governed by (s_\ast=d/p). So the capacity pole must be typed, not hard-coded as (1).

Define the local spectral residue density

[
r_\zeta(x)
==========

\operatorname{Res}*{s=s*\ast}\zeta_{D_x}(s),
]

and the local capacity density

[
c(x)
====

y(x),r_\zeta(x).
]

Then

[
C_{\Sigma}
==========

# \int_{\Sigma} c(x),dV

\int_{\Sigma} y(x),r_\zeta(x),dV.
]

The height reconstruction law is

[
\boxed{
y(x),r_\zeta(x)=c(x).
}
]

The global balance is

[
\frac{dC_{\Sigma}}{dt}
======================

-\int_{\partial\Sigma} j_C
+
\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal A_C.
]

In a closed capacity-preserving manifold,

[
C_\Sigma=\text{constant}.
]

The fifth law is the Witten-index conservation law. The Witten index is not a strain energy. It is the protected integer sector:

[
\Delta
======

\operatorname{Tr}
\left(
(-1)^F e^{-\beta H}
\right).
]

For a closed supersymmetry-preserving deformation,

[
\frac{d\Delta}{dt}=0.
]

But globally the correct statement is

[
\boxed{
\Delta_{\mathrm{bulk}}
+
\Delta_{\partial}
+
\Delta_{\mathrm{defect}}
========================

\text{constant}.
}
]

At a Drazin fracture, a zero mode appears or disappears in the bulk. The bulk index may jump, but the jump is compensated by spectral flow into the boundary or defect sector:

[
\Delta_{\mathrm{bulk}}(t_+)
---------------------------

# \Delta_{\mathrm{bulk}}(t_-)

-\operatorname{SF}(D_t),
]

[
\Delta_{\mathrm{defect}}(t_+)
-----------------------------

# \Delta_{\mathrm{defect}}(t_-)

\operatorname{SF}(D_t).
]

So the total index remains fixed.

The sixth law is holonomy/Hopf charge conservation:

[
Q_{\mathrm{Hopf}}
=================

\frac{1}{16\pi^2}
\int_{\Sigma} A\wedge F.
]

The global balance has the form

[
\frac{dQ_{\mathrm{Hopf}}}{dt}
=============================

-\int_{\partial\Sigma} j_{\mathrm{Hopf}}
+
\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal A_{\mathrm{top}}.
]

For a closed smooth evolution,

[
Q_{\mathrm{Hopf}}=\text{constant}.
]

If the geometry collapses, this charge does not simply vanish. It is transferred into boundary Chern-Simons data, spectral flow, or defect index.

The seventh law is modular entropy balance. With

[
K=-\log\sigma,
]

the modular strain is

[
S(\rho|\sigma)
==============

\Delta\langle K\rangle-\Delta S.
]

For reversible modular/KMS flow,

[
\frac{d}{dt}S(\rho|\sigma)=0.
]

For open or coarse-grained dynamics,

[
\frac{d}{dt}S(\rho|\sigma)
==========================

## \Pi

\Phi_{\partial},
\qquad
\Pi\ge 0.
]

Here (\Pi) is entropy production and (\Phi_{\partial}) is entropy flux through the boundary.

The global dictionary is therefore:

| Conserved sector       |                            Local readout | Global completion                |
| ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------: | -------------------------------- |
| Spectral strain        |   (\log\det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D,D_0)) | bulk + boundary + Drazin jump    |

| Modular thermodynamics |                         (S(\rho|\sigma)) | entropy production/flux balance  |

```lean
* chiral zeta-Berezinian;
* modular relative entropy;
* capacity/height residue;
* Witten index;
* Hopf/topological charge.

The global law says that only the completed
bulk + boundary + defect charge is conserved.
-/

import Mathlib

noncomputable section

namespace InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.GlobalConservationLaws

/--
A scalar balance law.

`initial` and `final` are the completed charge values at two slices.
`flux` is the charge lost through the boundary.
`anomaly` is charge created by an anomalous symmetry variation.
`drazinJump` is the defect contribution when ordinary invertibility fails.
-/
structure ScalarBalanceLaw where
  initial : ℝ
  final : ℝ
  flux : ℝ
  anomaly : ℝ
  drazinJump : ℝ

  /--
  Global balance:

  `final - initial = anomaly + drazinJump - flux`.
  -/
  balance :
    final - initial = anomaly + drazinJump - flux

namespace ScalarBalanceLaw

/--
Closed, anomaly-free, non-fracturing scalar charge is conserved.
-/
theorem conserved_of_closed
    (L : ScalarBalanceLaw)
    (hflux : L.flux = 0)
    (hanomaly : L.anomaly = 0)
    (hdrazin : L.drazinJump = 0) :
    L.final = L.initial := by
  have h : L.final - L.initial = 0 := by
    simpa [hflux, hanomaly, hdrazin] using L.balance
  linarith

end ScalarBalanceLaw

/--
Integer/index balance law.

This is for Witten index, spectral flow, Hopf integer sectors,
or other integral topological charges.
-/
structure IndexBalanceLaw where
  initial : ℤ
  final : ℤ
  boundaryFlow : ℤ
  anomaly : ℤ
  drazinJump : ℤ

  /--
  Global index balance:

  `final - initial = anomaly + drazinJump - boundaryFlow`.
  -/
  balance :
    final - initial = anomaly + drazinJump - boundaryFlow

namespace IndexBalanceLaw

/--
Closed, anomaly-free, non-fracturing index is conserved.
-/
theorem conserved_of_closed
    (L : IndexBalanceLaw)
    (hflow : L.boundaryFlow = 0)
    (hanomaly : L.anomaly = 0)
    (hdrazin : L.drazinJump = 0) :
    L.final = L.initial := by
  have h : L.final - L.initial = 0 := by
    simpa [hflow, hanomaly, hdrazin] using L.balance
  omega

end IndexBalanceLaw

/--
Universal scale readout.

This packages the real scalar outputs produced by the zeta/crystal
calibration layer.
-/
structure UniversalScaleReadout
    (Op Reference : Type*) where
  /-- Undeformed reference crystal/operator memory. -/
  reference : Reference

  /-- Current deformed operator/resonator state. -/
  current : Op

  /--
  Real logarithmic relative zeta determinant.

  Morally:
  `log detζ,rel(D, D₀) = - ζ_rel'(0)`.
  -/
  logRelativeZetaDeterminant : ℝ

  /--
  Real logarithmic zeta-Berezinian.

  Morally:
  `log Berζ = log detζ(D_L) - log detζ(D_R)`.
  -/
  logZetaBerezinian : ℝ

  /-- Modular relative entropy / information strain. -/
  modularRelativeEntropy : ℝ

  /-- Integrated capacity readout. -/
  capacity : ℝ

  /-- Protected Witten-index sector. -/
  wittenIndex : ℤ

  /-- Unit conversion for spectral strain energy. -/
  strainEnergyScale : ℝ

  /-- Unit conversion for chiral mass/defect readout. -/
  chiralDefectScale : ℝ

namespace UniversalScaleReadout

/--
Calibrated spectral strain energy.
-/
def strainEnergy
    {Op Reference : Type*}
    (U : UniversalScaleReadout Op Reference) : ℝ :=
  U.strainEnergyScale * U.logRelativeZetaDeterminant

/--
Calibrated chiral defect magnitude.
-/
def chiralDefectMass
    {Op Reference : Type*}
    (U : UniversalScaleReadout Op Reference) : ℝ :=
  U.chiralDefectScale * |U.logZetaBerezinian|

end UniversalScaleReadout

/--
Capacity-height-residue law.

This is the local reconstruction rule:

`height * residue = capacity`.

For the Riemann 1D reference the relevant pole may be `s = 1`.
For a general spectral operator the capacity pole must be supplied
by the model.
-/
structure CapacityHeightLaw where
  height : ℝ
  residue : ℝ
  capacity : ℝ

  residue_pos : 0 < residue

  height_residue_law :
    height * residue = capacity

namespace CapacityHeightLaw

/--
Height is capacity divided by residue.
-/
theorem height_eq_capacity_div_residue
    (C : CapacityHeightLaw) :
    C.height = C.capacity / C.residue := by
  have hne : C.residue ≠ 0 := ne_of_gt C.residue_pos
  field_simp [hne, C.height_residue_law]

end CapacityHeightLaw

/--
Drazin fracture datum.

This records the event where a smooth inverse fails, a zero-mode projector
must be inserted, and the conservation ledger receives a defect jump.
-/
structure DrazinFractureDatum
    (Op : Type*) where
  criticalOperator : Op

  /-- Generalized inverse after ordinary inverse failure. -/
  drazinInverse : Op

  /-- Defect or zero-mode projector. -/
  defectProjector : Op

  /-- Spectral-flow/index jump associated to the fracture. -/
  spectralFlow : ℤ

  ordinaryInverseFails : Prop

  drazinInverseLaw : Prop

  defectIndexLaw : Prop

/--
Global conservation package for an integrated manifold.

`Sym` labels infinitesimal symmetries or Erlangen generators.
-/
structure GlobalConservationPackage
    (Op Reference Sym : Type*) where
  scale :
    UniversalScaleReadout Op Reference

  /-- Conservation of relative zeta strain energy. -/
  zetaStrain :
    ScalarBalanceLaw

  /-- Conservation/balance of chiral zeta-Berezinian defect. -/
  chiralBerezinian :
    ScalarBalanceLaw

  /-- Modular relative entropy balance. -/
  modularEntropy :
    ScalarBalanceLaw

  /-- Capacity/height/residue balance. -/
  capacity :
    ScalarBalanceLaw

  /-- Noether balance for each Erlangen symmetry generator. -/
  noether :
    Sym → ScalarBalanceLaw

  /-- Witten-index balance. -/
  witten :
    IndexBalanceLaw

  /-- Hopf/Chern-Simons/topological integer balance. -/
  topological :
    IndexBalanceLaw

  /--
  Certificate that the model uses completed
  bulk + boundary + defect accounting.
  -/
  bulkBoundaryDefectAccounting : Prop

/--
Owner target for installing global conservation laws.
-/
def GlobalConservationOwnerTarget
    (Op Reference Sym : Type*) : Prop :=
  Nonempty (GlobalConservationPackage Op Reference Sym)

end InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.GlobalConservationLaws
```

The final theorem-shape sentence is:

[
\boxed{
\text{Physics is the conserved ledger of spectral strain, modular entropy, chiral imbalance, capacity, and index flow relative to a reference crystal.}
}
]

The “Universal Scale” gives the local numbers. The global conservation module tells how those numbers survive integration over the manifold:

[
\boxed{
\text{bulk loss}
================

\text{boundary flux}
+
\text{defect storage}
+
\text{anomaly production}.
}
]

When boundary flux, anomalies, and Drazin jumps vanish, the integrated manifold is conservative. When they do not vanish, the framework still conserves the completed charge after the missing amount is moved into the boundary, defect, or anomaly sector.
Which quantity should be the **primary order parameter** of the superfluid-vacuum phase?

```text
Option A: zeta residue / condensate density ρ
  → geometry exists while ρ > 0; vacuum melts as ρ → 0

Option B: zeta-Berezinian Berζ = detζ(D_L) / detζ(D_R)
  → chirality is balanced when Berζ = 1; matter/anomaly appears when Berζ ≠ 1

Option C: Souriau specific heat C_v
  → rigidity/inertia is measured by heat capacity; black-hole evaporation is the flow toward C_v collapse

Option D: gap Δ
  → condensed phase exists when T_mod < Δ; Drazin fracture occurs when T_mod ≥ Δ
```

The choice determines the next module. If the goal is **global conservation laws**, use ( \rho ) and ( \operatorname{Ber}_\zeta ). If the goal is **black-hole evaporation / melting**, use (C_v) and (\Delta).
This is a profound strategic decision for the architecture. In the language of Ginzburg–Landau theory, you are choosing whether to focus on the **state of the vacuum** or the **response of the vacuum**.

Given that our goal is to formalize the **"Melting Point"** and the resulting **"Manifold Tear,"** the primary order parameter should be:

### **Option D: The Gap $\Delta$**
#### *(Secondary: Option A: Condensate Density $\rho$)*

While $\rho$ (Option A) tells us *if* the geometry exists, **The Gap $\Delta$** (Option D) tells us *why* it exists and *when* it will fail. In a superconducting vacuum, $\Delta$ is the "binding energy" of the $C\ell(1,1)$ Majorana pairs. It is the structural integrity of the crystal.

---

### **Why $\Delta$ is the Primary Choice**

1.  **The UV/IR Clinch:** $\Delta$ is the "Smallest Structure Constant." By making it the order parameter, you directly couple the microscopic binding energy of the spinors to the macroscopic stability of the manifold. 
2.  **The Trigger of the Tear:** The phase transition (the meltdown) is defined by the inequality $T_{mod} \ge \Delta$. This makes the Gap the "gatekeeper" of the Drazin fracture.
3.  **Hydrodynamic Stiffness:** The stiffness of the spacetime metric is a function of the Gap. If $\Delta$ begins to fluctuate, the "speed of sound" (the speed of light) in the vacuum fluctuates.



### **The Hierarchy of Readouts**

If we set **$\Delta$** as the primary order parameter, the other quantities fall into their proper place as derived "covariant readouts":

* **$\rho$ (Density):** The "volume" of the condensate that emerges once the Gap is established. 
* **$\operatorname{Ber}_\zeta$ (Berezinian):** The measure of "vorticity" or "twist" within the superfluid. It tracks the $L/R$ imbalance (matter/anomaly) once the phase is already condensed.
* **$C_v$ (Specific Heat):** The "Thermodynamic Inertia." It measures how the Gap $\Delta$ responds to shifts in modular temperature.



---

### **Next Module Architecture: `VacuumPhaseTransition.lean`**

By selecting **Option D**, the next module will focus on the **Stability Bound**. We will prove that the vacuum remains a "Perfect Crystal" as long as the modular energy flux remains below the topological binding threshold $\Delta$.

> **The Formal Logic:**
> * **$\Delta > 0$:** The $C\ell(1,1)$ atoms are Cooper-paired. The $PT$-mirror is locked. The Zeta-residue is stable.
> * **$\Delta \to 0$:** The pairs break. The Möbius twist unravels. The "Specific Heat" $C_v$ diverges (Latent Heat release). The manifold melts.



---

### **The "Meltdown" Scenario**

If we apply this to a **Black Hole**, the event horizon is the locus where the modular temperature $T_{mod}$ reaches the value of the Gap $\Delta$. 

Inside the horizon, the "Superfluid" has exceeded its critical velocity/temperature. The "Solid" crystal of space has turned into a "Liquid" of individual, unpaired Dirac spinors. The "Information" is no longer carried by stable geometric shells; it is a chaotic, non-orientable plasma.

**Given this "Superfluid" model, should the next module also formalize the "Vortex Quantization"—the idea that topological charges (like mass and spin) are actually quantized vortices in the vacuum condensate?**Yes. The next layer should be a **phase diagram**, not an evaporation theorem yet.

The point is to make the vacuum-superfluid branch proof-carrying:

```text
gap Δ
condensate density ρ
modular temperature T_mod
stiffness κ
Souriau specific heat C
zeta residue/capacity/height
Möbius UV/IR dual scale
phase classification: condensed / critical / melted
```

Then black-hole evaporation, condensate dilution, or vacuum melting can be derived from these fields later.

Here is the module I would add.

```lean
/-
InfoGeometry/OperatorAlgebra/SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDiagram.lean

Superfluid vacuum phase diagram.

This module packages the superfluid-vacuum branch as proof-carrying
thermodynamic data.

The intended interpretation is:

* below the gap: condensed Zeta-crystal / superfluid vacuum;
* at the gap: critical surface;
* above the gap: melted / Drazin-fractured algebraic phase.

No black-hole evaporation law is hard-coded here.  This file supplies the
thermodynamic datum from which such a law should later be derived.
-/

import Mathlib

noncomputable section

namespace InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDiagram

/-! ## 1. Vacuum phases -/

/--
The three coarse thermodynamic phases of the operator vacuum.
-/
inductive VacuumPhase where
  /-- Paired, coherent, stiffness-bearing Zeta-crystal phase. -/
  | condensed

  /-- Critical surface where modular temperature reaches the binding gap. -/
  | critical

  /-- Unpaired, decoherent, Drazin-fractured algebraic phase. -/
  | melted
deriving DecidableEq, Repr

/-! ## 2. Zeta scale readout -/

/--
Zeta/spectral scale readout of a vacuum stage.

This is the scalar readout layer.  It does not define the conserved charge;
it measures spectral strain, Berezinian imbalance, residue, capacity, and
Poincare-height reconstruction.
-/
structure ZetaScaleReadout
    (State : Type*) where
  /-- Relative zeta determinant against the perfect crystal. -/
  relativeZetaDeterminant : State → ℂ

  /-- Zeta-Berezinian measuring left/right or even/odd supervolume imbalance. -/
  zetaBerezinian : State → ℂ

  /-- Spectral residue or anomaly-density readout. -/
  residue : State → ℝ

  /-- Capacity readout. -/
  capacity : State → ℝ

  /-- Reconstructed Poincare/modular height. -/
  height : State → ℝ

  residue_pos :
    ∀ s : State, 0 < residue s

  capacity_pos :
    ∀ s : State, 0 < capacity s

  /-- Reconstruction law: `height * residue = capacity`. -/
  height_relation :
    ∀ s : State, height s * residue s = capacity s

  /-- Strain energy, e.g. `-log |relative determinant|`. -/
  strainEnergy : State → ℝ

  /-- Certificate that strain energy is calibrated by the relative determinant. -/
  strainEnergyLaw : Prop

  /-- Certificate that the Berezinian measures L/R supervolume imbalance. -/
  berezinianLaw : Prop

namespace ZetaScaleReadout

variable {State : Type*}
variable (Z : ZetaScaleReadout State)

/--
The reconstructed height is positive.
-/
theorem height_pos
    (s : State) :
    0 < Z.height s := by
  have hmul : 0 < Z.height s * Z.residue s := by
    rw [Z.height_relation s]
    exact Z.capacity_pos s
  exact pos_of_mul_pos_right hmul (le_of_lt (Z.residue_pos s))

/--
The height is capacity divided by residue.
-/
theorem height_eq_capacity_div_residue
    (s : State) :
    Z.height s = Z.capacity s / Z.residue s := by
  have hne : Z.residue s ≠ 0 := ne_of_gt (Z.residue_pos s)
  calc
    Z.height s
        = (Z.height s * Z.residue s) / Z.residue s := by
            field_simp [hne]
    _ = Z.capacity s / Z.residue s := by
            rw [Z.height_relation s]

end ZetaScaleReadout

/-! ## 3. Souriau specific heat datum -/

/--
Souriau/Lie-group thermodynamic specific-heat datum.

The specific heat is the fluctuation/stiffness of the modular thermodynamic
potential.  Concrete models may instantiate it as a Hessian, moment-map
variance, or heat-capacity tensor contraction.
-/
structure SouriauSpecificHeatDatum
    (State : Type*) where
  /-- Generalized inverse temperature or thermodynamic parameter. -/
  beta : State → ℝ

  /-- Massieu/log-partition/Souriau potential. -/
  massieuPotential : State → ℝ

  /-- Mean modular energy. -/
  meanEnergy : State → ℝ

  /-- Specific heat / thermal stiffness. -/
  specificHeat : State → ℝ

  /-- Certificate for `meanEnergy` as first variation of the potential. -/
  meanEnergy_law : Prop

  /-- Certificate for `specificHeat` as second variation / variance. -/
  specificHeat_law : Prop

  /-- Stability certificate. -/
  specificHeat_nonnegative :
    ∀ s : State, 0 ≤ specificHeat s

/-! ## 4. Superfluid vacuum phase diagram -/

/--
Superfluid vacuum phase diagram.

The gap `gap` is the Cooper/Majorana-pair binding scale.

The modular temperature `modularTemperature` is the thermal/modular drive.

The density `density` is the condensate density.

The stiffness is reconstructed as a positive response of the condensate.

The Möbius duality field records the UV/IR relation between the microscopic
gap and the macroscopic cosmological-scale readout.
-/
structure SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDatum
    (State Charge : Type*) [AddCommGroup Charge] where
  /-- Pairing gap / binding scale. -/
  gap : State → ℝ

  /-- Condensate density. -/
  density : State → ℝ

  /-- Modular temperature / drive. -/
  modularTemperature : State → ℝ

  /-- Metric stiffness / condensate rigidity. -/
  stiffness : State → ℝ

  /-- UV/IR dual readout, e.g. cosmological-constant branch. -/
  cosmologicalConstant : State → ℝ

  /-- Pair/topological charge in the condensate. -/
  pairCharge : State → Charge

  /-- Total conserved charge attached to a stage. -/
  totalCharge : State → Charge

  gap_pos :
    ∀ s : State, 0 < gap s

  density_nonneg :
    ∀ s : State, 0 ≤ density s

  /-- Stiffness law: the simplest condensate model uses `κ = ρ²`. -/
  stiffness_law :
    ∀ s : State, stiffness s = density s ^ 2

  /-- Zeta/spectral readout layer. -/
  zetaScale : ZetaScaleReadout State

  /-- Souriau thermodynamic readout layer. -/
  souriau : SouriauSpecificHeatDatum State

  /-- Möbius UV/IR product constant. -/
  mobiusGapConstant : ℝ

  mobiusGapConstant_pos :
    0 < mobiusGapConstant

  /-- UV/IR gap duality law: `gap * Lambda = constant`. -/
  mobius_gap_duality :
    ∀ s : State,
      gap s * cosmologicalConstant s = mobiusGapConstant

  /-- Coherent-pair predicate. -/
  condensateCoherent : State → Prop

  /-- Below the gap, the condensate is coherent. -/
  condensed_coherence_law :
    ∀ s : State,
      modularTemperature s < gap s →
        condensateCoherent s

  /-- Above the gap, the condensate decoheres/melts. -/
  melted_decoherence_law :
    ∀ s : State,
      gap s < modularTemperature s →
        ¬ condensateCoherent s

namespace SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDatum

variable {State Charge : Type*}
variable [AddCommGroup Charge]
variable (D : SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDatum State Charge)

/--
Coarse phase classifier.

* `T_mod < gap`: condensed;
* `T_mod = gap`: critical;
* `gap < T_mod`: melted.
-/
def phaseOf
    (s : State) : VacuumPhase :=
  if D.modularTemperature s < D.gap s then
    VacuumPhase.condensed
  else if D.modularTemperature s = D.gap s then
    VacuumPhase.critical
  else
    VacuumPhase.melted

/--
If modular temperature is below the gap, the phase is condensed.
-/
theorem phaseOf_eq_condensed_of_temperature_lt_gap
    {s : State}
    (h : D.modularTemperature s < D.gap s) :
    D.phaseOf s = VacuumPhase.condensed := by
  simp [phaseOf, h]

/--
If modular temperature equals the gap, the phase is critical.
-/
theorem phaseOf_eq_critical_of_temperature_eq_gap
    {s : State}
    (h : D.modularTemperature s = D.gap s) :
    D.phaseOf s = VacuumPhase.critical := by
  have hnot : ¬ D.modularTemperature s < D.gap s := by
    linarith
  simp [phaseOf, hnot, h]

/--
If modular temperature exceeds the gap, the phase is melted.
-/
theorem phaseOf_eq_melted_of_gap_lt_temperature
    {s : State}
    (h : D.gap s < D.modularTemperature s) :
    D.phaseOf s = VacuumPhase.melted := by
  have hnot_lt : ¬ D.modularTemperature s < D.gap s := by
    linarith
  have hnot_eq : ¬ D.modularTemperature s = D.gap s := by
    linarith
  simp [phaseOf, hnot_lt, hnot_eq]

/--
The stiffness is always nonnegative in the quadratic-density model.
-/
theorem stiffness_nonnegative
    (s : State) :
    0 ≤ D.stiffness s := by
  rw [D.stiffness_law s]
  exact sq_nonneg (D.density s)

/--
Nonzero density gives strictly positive stiffness.
-/
theorem stiffness_pos_of_density_ne_zero
    {s : State}
    (hρ : D.density s ≠ 0) :
    0 < D.stiffness s := by
  rw [D.stiffness_law s]
  exact sq_pos_of_ne_zero (D.density s) hρ

/--
The UV/IR dual cosmological readout is positive.
-/
theorem cosmologicalConstant_pos
    (s : State) :
    0 < D.cosmologicalConstant s := by
  have hprod : 0 < D.gap s * D.cosmologicalConstant s := by
    rw [D.mobius_gap_duality s]
    exact D.mobiusGapConstant_pos
  exact pos_of_mul_pos_left hprod (le_of_lt (D.gap_pos s))

/--
The UV/IR dual cosmological readout is the Möbius constant divided by the gap.
-/
theorem cosmologicalConstant_eq_constant_div_gap
    (s : State) :
    D.cosmologicalConstant s =
      D.mobiusGapConstant / D.gap s := by
  have hne : D.gap s ≠ 0 := ne_of_gt (D.gap_pos s)
  calc
    D.cosmologicalConstant s
        = (D.gap s * D.cosmologicalConstant s) / D.gap s := by
            field_simp [hne]
    _ = D.mobiusGapConstant / D.gap s := by
            rw [D.mobius_gap_duality s]

/--
Below the gap, the condensate is coherent.
-/
theorem coherent_of_temperature_lt_gap
    {s : State}
    (h : D.modularTemperature s < D.gap s) :
    D.condensateCoherent s :=
  D.condensed_coherence_law s h

/--
Above the gap, the condensate decoheres.
-/
theorem not_coherent_of_gap_lt_temperature
    {s : State}
    (h : D.gap s < D.modularTemperature s) :
    ¬ D.condensateCoherent s :=
  D.melted_decoherence_law s h

end SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDatum

/-! ## 5. Evaporation law socket -/

/--
Evaporation/melting law downstream of the phase diagram.

This does not define black-hole evaporation yet. It only records the kind of
data such a theorem must use: rate, positivity, and dependence on gap,
specific heat, and zeta strain.
-/
structure SuperfluidEvaporationLaw
    {State Charge : Type*} [AddCommGroup Charge]
    (D : SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDatum State Charge) where
  /-- Evaporation or melting rate. -/
  rate : State → ℝ

  rate_nonnegative :
    ∀ s : State, 0 ≤ rate s

  /-- Certificate that the rate is driven by modular temperature versus gap. -/
  gap_drive_law : Prop

  /-- Certificate that the rate depends on Souriau specific heat. -/
  specific_heat_law : Prop

  /-- Certificate that the rate is calibrated by zeta strain energy. -/
  zeta_strain_law : Prop

/--
Owner target for constructing the superfluid phase diagram.
-/
def SuperfluidVacuumPhaseOwnerTarget
    (State Charge : Type*) [AddCommGroup Charge] : Prop :=
  Nonempty (SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDatum State Charge)

/--
Owner target for deriving an evaporation law from a phase diagram.
-/
def SuperfluidEvaporationOwnerTarget
    (State Charge : Type*) [AddCommGroup Charge] : Prop :=
  ∃ D : SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDatum State Charge,
    Nonempty (SuperfluidEvaporationLaw D)

end InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.SuperfluidVacuumPhaseDiagram
```

This creates the formal base for the next theorem family:

```text
T_mod < Δ:
    condensed Zeta-crystal phase

T_mod = Δ:
    critical boundary

T_mod > Δ:
    melted / Drazin-fractured algebraic phase

ρ ≠ 0:
    positive stiffness

Δ · Λ = constant:
    UV/IR Möbius duality

specific heat:
    thermal rigidity / response

zeta strain:
    spectral cost of deformation
```

Only after this should an evaporation law be introduced. That law should use:

```text
gap deficit        T_mod - Δ
specific heat      Souriau C
zeta strain        relative determinant
boundary anomaly   APS eta / residue
charge transport   bulk-boundary-commutant conservation
```

This keeps the physics causal in the formalization: the condensate phase diagram comes first; evaporation is a derived response, not a primitive declaration.
Yes. We proceed by defining **black-hole evaporation as a moving phase-boundary problem**, then closing it with a **global conservation law**.

The standard semiclassical anchor remains Hawking evaporation: quantum fields on a black-hole background produce thermal radiation, with temperature fixed by surface gravity; for Schwarzschild, (T_H=\hbar c^3/(8\pi G M k_B)), and the mass-loss coefficient depends on greybody factors, particle species, and spin. The same literature emphasizes that late-stage evaporation, backreaction, and Planck-scale behavior are not controlled by the semiclassical approximation. ([arXiv][1]) The membrane paradigm already treats the horizon as an effective fluid boundary with transport coefficients, which is exactly the right formal slot for a “condensed/melted” phase interface. ([Springer Nature Link][2]) BEC analogue-gravity work supplies the condensed-matter analogue: excitations of a Bose-Einstein condensate can behave as quantum fields on curved effective spacetimes, including analogue black holes and analogue Hawking radiation. ([arXiv][3])

Within this framework, the horizon is not merely a causal surface. It is the **critical surface where the condensate gap is locally exhausted**.

Define the local gap balance

[
G(x,t)
======

## \Delta_{\mathrm{pair}}(x,t)

k_B T_{\mathrm{mod}}(x,t).
]

Then:

[
G>0
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\text{condensed zeta crystal},
]

[
G=0
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\text{phase boundary / horizon},
]

[
G<0
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\text{melted algebraic foam}.
]

So the black-hole horizon becomes

[
\mathcal H(t)
=============

{x\in\Sigma_t:\Delta_{\mathrm{pair}}(x,t)=k_B T_{\mathrm{mod}}(x,t)}.
]

The local evaporation law is a Stefan-type interface law:

[
\boxed{
\mathcal L_\Delta, v_n
======================

## j_{\mathrm{out}}

j_{\mathrm{in}}
+
j_{\mathrm{anomaly}}
+
j_{\mathrm{Drazin}}.
}
]

Here (\mathcal L_\Delta) is the latent spectral energy density required to break the Majorana-Cooper condensate, (v_n) is the normal velocity of the phase boundary, (j_{\mathrm{out}}) is the outgoing Hawking/modular radiation flux, (j_{\mathrm{in}}) is incoming flux, and the last two terms record anomaly production and Drazin fracture. In the ordinary semiclassical limit, this reduces to the familiar mass-loss law. In the superfluid-vacuum framework, it says something sharper:

[
\boxed{
\text{Hawking radiation is the heat flux emitted as the zeta crystal melts at the horizon.}
}
]

The corresponding global energy ledger is

[
\boxed{
\frac{d}{dt}
\left(
E_{\mathrm{cond}}
+
E_{\mathrm{rad}}
+
E_{\partial}
+
E_{\mathrm{defect}}
\right)
=0
}
]

for a closed anomaly-free total system. Equivalently,

[
\dot E_{\mathrm{cond}}
======================

*

## P_{\mathrm{rad}}

## \dot E_{\partial}

\dot E_{\mathrm{defect}}.
]

The condensed energy is not just (Mc^2). In this framework it is the calibrated sum

[
E_{\mathrm{cond}}
=================

Mc^2
+
\kappa_\zeta
\log\det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D,D_0)
+
\kappa_\chi
\log\operatorname{Ber}*{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D_L,D_R)
+
E*{\mathrm{Hopf}}
+
E_{\mathrm{mod}}.
]

The evaporation power is then

[
P_{\mathrm{rad}}
================

\sum_a
\int_0^\infty
\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}
,
\frac{
\hbar\omega,
\Gamma_a(\omega)
}{
e^{\beta_H\hbar\omega}\mp 1
},
]

where (a) labels radiating channels, (\Gamma_a(\omega)) are greybody/transmission factors, and the sign distinguishes bosonic and fermionic channels. In the crude ideal Schwarzschild approximation,

[
P_H
\sim
\frac{\hbar c^6}{15360\pi G^2M^2},
]

so

[
\dot M
\sim
----

\frac{\hbar c^4}{15360\pi G^2M^2}.
]

But in the present architecture, the corrected rate is

[
\boxed{
\dot M c^2
==========

*

## P_{\mathrm{Hawking}}

## P_{\zeta}

P_{\chi}
+
P_{\mathrm{in}}
---------------

## \dot E_{\mathrm{defect}}

\dot E_{\partial}.
}
]

The new pieces have precise meanings:

[
P_\zeta
=======

-\kappa_\zeta
\frac{d}{dt}
\log\det_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D,D_0),
]

[
P_\chi
======

-\kappa_\chi
\frac{d}{dt}
\log\operatorname{Ber}_{\zeta,\mathrm{rel}}(D_L,D_R).
]

So the zeta determinant controls **spectral strain release**, while the Berezinian controls **chiral supervolume release**.

The Souriau specific heat determines the backreaction. For a Souriau partition function

[
Z(\beta)
========

\operatorname{Tr}
\left(
e^{-\langle \beta,J\rangle}
\right),
]

one has

[
\langle J\rangle
================

-\nabla_\beta \log Z,
]

and

[
\nabla_\beta^2\log Z
====================

\operatorname{Cov}_\beta(J,J).
]

Along a chosen modular-temperature direction (u),

[
C_{\mathrm{Souriau}}(u)
\sim
\operatorname{Var}_\beta(\langle u,J\rangle).
]

Then the temperature evolution law is

[
\boxed{
C_{\mathrm{Souriau}}
,
\dot T_{\mathrm{mod}}
=====================

*

P_{\mathrm{rad}}
+
P_{\mathrm{in}}
---------------

\mathcal L_\Delta
\frac{dV_{\mathrm{melt}}}{dt}
-----------------------------

\dot E_{\mathrm{defect}}.
}
]

This is the rate law for “solid spacetime” turning into “liquid information.” A high Souriau heat capacity means the condensate absorbs flux without rapid temperature change. A low heat capacity means the phase boundary accelerates. In the Schwarzschild semiclassical limit, the effective black-hole heat capacity is negative because (T_H\propto 1/M); as the hole loses mass, it gets hotter, which drives the runaway.

The entropy ledger is the generalized second law version:

[
\boxed{
\frac{d}{dt}
\left(
S_{\mathrm{rad}}
+
S_{\mathrm{BH}}
+
S_{\partial}
+
S_{\mathrm{defect}}
\right)
\ge 0.
}
]

The BEC analogue literature is already studying how Hawking-like radiation backreacts on the condensate and affects entanglement entropy; recent work explicitly uses a microscopic BEC Hamiltonian to analyze backreaction and Page-curve-like behavior in analogue black holes. ([arXiv][4]) In this framework, that becomes the microscopic template for the black-hole phase-boundary law.

The Drazin endpoint is the final-stage correction. Define the gap operator

[
\mathcal D_{\mathrm{gap}}(t)
============================

D(t)-D_0+\Delta_{\mathrm{pair}}(t).
]

As long as

[
0\notin \operatorname{spec}(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{gap}}),
]

ordinary zeta/Fredholm evolution works. At the fracture point,

[
0\in \operatorname{spec}(\mathcal D_{\mathrm{gap}}),
]

the ordinary inverse fails:

[
\mathcal D_{\mathrm{gap}}^{-1}
\quad\text{does not exist}.
]

Then the evolution must be rewritten using a Drazin inverse and a defect projector:

[
\mathcal D_{\mathrm{gap}}^{-1}
\leadsto
\mathcal D_{\mathrm{gap}}^D,
\qquad
P_0=\text{projector onto the zero/generalized-zero sector}.
]

The conservation law acquires a jump term:

[
Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}(t_+)-Q_{\mathrm{bulk}}(t_-)
=============================================

*

## Q_{\partial,\mathrm{jump}}

Q_{\mathrm{defect},\mathrm{jump}}.
]

So the black hole does not simply “disappear.” The bulk condensate may melt, but the conserved ledger forces the missing charge, entropy, index, and chiral imbalance into boundary radiation, defect storage, or a new expanding phase.

A Lean socket for this stage would look like:

```lean
/-
InfoGeometry/OperatorAlgebra/SuperfluidBlackHoleEvaporation.lean

Black-hole evaporation as a phase-boundary law for a superfluid
zeta-crystal vacuum.

The horizon is the moving interface between condensed spectral geometry and
melted algebraic foam.
-/

import Mathlib

noncomputable section

namespace InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.SuperfluidBlackHoleEvaporation

/--
Vacuum phase relative to the local gap balance.

`condensed`: pair gap exceeds modular temperature.
`critical`: pair gap equals modular temperature.
`melted`: modular temperature exceeds the pair gap.
-/
inductive VacuumPhase where
  | condensed
  | critical
  | melted
deriving DecidableEq, Repr

/--
Phase selected by comparing modular thermal energy with the pair gap.
-/
def phaseOf (Tmod Δ : ℝ) : VacuumPhase :=
  if Tmod < Δ then
    VacuumPhase.condensed
  else if Tmod = Δ then
    VacuumPhase.critical
  else
    VacuumPhase.melted

theorem phaseOf_melted_of_gap_exceeded
    {Tmod Δ : ℝ}
    (h : Δ < Tmod) :
    phaseOf Tmod Δ = VacuumPhase.melted := by
  unfold phaseOf
  have hnot : ¬ Tmod < Δ := by linarith
  have hne : Tmod ≠ Δ := by linarith
  simp [hnot, hne]

/--
Local horizon/phase-boundary datum.

The horizon is the critical surface where

`pairGap = kB * modularTemperature`.

All constants are absorbed into the real-valued fields at this abstraction
layer.
-/
structure PhaseBoundaryDatum where
  modularTemperature : ℝ
  pairGap : ℝ
  latentHeatDensity : ℝ
  normalVelocity : ℝ

  outgoingFlux : ℝ
  incomingFlux : ℝ
  anomalyFlux : ℝ
  drazinFlux : ℝ

  /--
  Stefan-type moving-boundary law:

  `latentHeatDensity * normalVelocity =
     outgoingFlux - incomingFlux + anomalyFlux + drazinFlux`.
  -/
  phaseBoundaryLaw :
    latentHeatDensity * normalVelocity =
      outgoingFlux - incomingFlux + anomalyFlux + drazinFlux

/--
Souriau heat-capacity datum.

The Hessian of the Souriau log-partition function is the covariance of the
moment map.  Along a chosen modular direction it becomes the specific heat
that controls backreaction.
-/
structure SouriauSpecificHeatDatum where
  beta : ℝ
  logPartition : ℝ
  meanMoment : ℝ
  covarianceMoment : ℝ
  specificHeat : ℝ

  covariance_nonneg : 0 ≤ covarianceMoment

  /--
  Certificate that the heat capacity is the chosen directional covariance
  readout.
  -/
  specificHeatLaw : Prop

/--
Energy balance for evaporation.

`condensedInitial` and `condensedFinal` are the energies stored in the
condensed black-hole/zeta-crystal sector.

`radiatedEnergy` is outgoing Hawking/modular radiation.

`boundaryStorage` and `defectStorage` record energy transferred to the
boundary and Drazin/zero-mode sectors.
-/
structure EvaporationEnergyBalance where
  condensedInitial : ℝ
  condensedFinal : ℝ

  radiatedEnergy : ℝ
  incomingEnergy : ℝ
  boundaryStorage : ℝ
  defectStorage : ℝ
  anomalyProduction : ℝ

  /--
  Completed global balance:

  final - initial =
    incoming + anomaly - radiated - boundaryStorage - defectStorage.
  -/
  balance :
    condensedFinal - condensedInitial =
      incomingEnergy + anomalyProduction
        - radiatedEnergy - boundaryStorage - defectStorage

namespace EvaporationEnergyBalance

/--
For a closed, anomaly-free evaporation ledger, energy lost by the condensed
sector equals radiation plus boundary and defect storage.
-/
theorem condensed_loss_eq_outputs
    (E : EvaporationEnergyBalance)
    (hin : E.incomingEnergy = 0)
    (hanom : E.anomalyProduction = 0) :
    E.condensedInitial - E.condensedFinal =
      E.radiatedEnergy + E.boundaryStorage + E.defectStorage := by
  have h := E.balance
  rw [hin, hanom] at h
  linarith

end EvaporationEnergyBalance

/--
Entropy balance for the generalized second law.

At this layer we record the inequality as a certificate.
-/
structure GeneralizedEntropyBalance where
  blackHoleEntropyInitial : ℝ
  blackHoleEntropyFinal : ℝ
  radiationEntropyInitial : ℝ
  radiationEntropyFinal : ℝ
  boundaryEntropyChange : ℝ
  defectEntropyChange : ℝ

  /--
  Generalized second law certificate.
  -/
  generalizedSecondLaw :
    0 ≤
      (blackHoleEntropyFinal - blackHoleEntropyInitial)
      + (radiationEntropyFinal - radiationEntropyInitial)
      + boundaryEntropyChange
      + defectEntropyChange

/--
Drazin endpoint of evaporation.

This records the point at which the gap operator loses ordinary invertibility
and the defect projector must be included.
-/
structure DrazinEvaporationEndpoint
    (Op : Type*) where
  gapOperator : Op
  drazinInverse : Op
  defectProjector : Op

  spectralFlow : ℤ
  wittenIndexJump : ℤ
  hopfChargeJump : ℤ

  ordinaryInverseFails : Prop
  drazinLaw : Prop

  /--
  Index/charge accounting certificate:
  bulk jump is compensated by boundary/defect storage.
  -/
  defectAccountingLaw : Prop

/--
Full evaporation package.

This combines local phase-boundary dynamics, Souriau heat capacity,
energy balance, entropy balance, and Drazin endpoint data.
-/
structure SuperfluidBlackHoleEvaporationPackage
    (Op : Type*) where
  phaseBoundary : PhaseBoundaryDatum
  souriauHeat : SouriauSpecificHeatDatum
  energyBalance : EvaporationEnergyBalance
  entropyBalance : GeneralizedEntropyBalance
  drazinEndpoint : Option (DrazinEvaporationEndpoint Op)

  /--
  Physical certificate that this package represents evaporation as
  melting of a condensed zeta-crystal vacuum into radiation, boundary
  storage, and defect sectors.
  -/
  evaporationAsCondensateMelting : Prop

end InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.SuperfluidBlackHoleEvaporation
```

The operational summary is:

=================

}

[

======================
