```text
    protected index in each BPS/central-charge sector
```

The compact theorem-shape sentence is:

[
\boxed{
\text{The Bilingual vacuum is a real }Cl(1,1)^n
\text{ supergeometry whose odd Majorana supercharges close onto}
\ P_\mu
\text{ and a central topological charge matrix } Z^{AB}.
}
]

And the physical readout is:

[
\boxed{
\text{BPS saturation}
=====================

\text{gap-protected coherent defect;}
\qquad
\text{Witten index}
===================

\text{protected count of such defects.}
}
]

This is the right way to make the framework “(N=4)-supergravity-like” without prematurely claiming a full physical (N=4) supergravity theory.

[1]: https://arxiv.org/pdf/hep-th/9507048 "arXiv:hep-th/9507048v3  26 Aug 1995"
[2]: https://arxiv.org/pdf/0903.2481 "arXiv:0903.2481v2  [hep-th]  12 May 2010"


Yes. That is the next structural layer: the **real Majorana superalgebra** sitting on top of the (Cl(1,1)^{\widehat\otimes 4}) chiral tensor.

The clean interpretation is:

[
\boxed{
\text{The }Cl(1,1)^4\text{ Cartan hypercube gives the real chiral sectoring; the supercharges are odd operators moving between those sectors.}
}
]

So the supergeometry is not added afterward. It is the functorial geometry of the odd maps between the even projector sectors.

In ordinary supersymmetry, the algebra extends the Poincaré algebra by fermionic generators (Q_\alpha), and extended supersymmetry has (Q^I_\alpha) with (I=1,\dots,N). Standard (N)-extended supersymmetry also allows central charges in the extended case; those central charges control BPS bounds and shortened multiplets. 

For your real-Majorana version:

```text
even algebra:
    geometry, projectors, rotations, bosonic observables

odd algebra:
    real Majorana supercharges, chiral supercharges, vortex/defect transport

central charges:
    topological charges, winding, Pfaffian sign, APS/eta boundary charge,
    Drazin nil-sector obstruction

Witten index:
    supertrace that survives deformation
```

The important refinement is that the (N=4) analogy has two meanings.

First, (N=4) supersymmetry in four dimensions has four independent supersymmetry generators in the Weyl-spinor notation, corresponding to sixteen real supercharges; (N=4) super-Yang–Mills has (SU(4)_R) R-symmetry and can be obtained by dimensional reduction from ten-dimensional (N=1) super-Yang–Mills. ([Wikipedia][1])

Second, your (Cl(1,1)^4) construction gives four **Cartan/chiral atoms**, hence a (\mathbb Z_2^4) sectoring:

[
H_i^2=1,\qquad [H_i,H_j]=0,\qquad i,j=1,\dots,4.
]

Those are not automatically the same thing as the four (N=4) supercharges. The correct bridge is a calibration datum:

```text
four Clifford atoms
    → four Cartan signs / chiral addresses

four Majorana supercharge families
    → odd maps between those chiral addresses

N=4-like branch
    → the supercharges act equivariantly on the Cl(1,1)^4 sector lattice
```

The supercharges should be **odd** with respect to the total chirality

[
\Gamma=H_1H_2H_3H_4.
]

That means

[
\Gamma Q = -Q\Gamma.
]

Consequently, (Q) flips total chirality:

[
Q P_L = P_R Q,\qquad Q P_R=P_L Q.
]

This is the exact analogue of the earlier modular mirror relation (J\chi=-\chi J), but now (Q) is not a mirror; it is a supercharge. It transports states between bosonic/fermionic or left/right sectors.

The Witten index is the invariant that counts what survives the odd pairing. In supersymmetric quantum mechanics and quantum field theory it is the graded trace

[
\operatorname{Tr}\left((-1)^F e^{-\beta H}\right),
]

and, because nonzero-energy bosonic and fermionic states pair under supersymmetry, it is insensitive to many continuous deformations and detects unpaired supersymmetric ground states. ([Wikipedia][2])

In your language:

```text
ordinary partition function:
    counts all thermal modes

Witten index:
    counts unpaired topological/supersymmetric modes

zeta determinant:
    regularizes infinite spectral product

Berezinian:
    ratio of even/odd or L/R spectral volumes

Pfaffian:
    real Majorana fermionic amplitude

central charge:
    topological obstruction carried by vortices, defects, boundary eta, or Pfaffian sign
```

So the Witten index is the **protected supertrace** of the chiral resonator. It ignores paired noise and remembers the unpaired topological core.

The central charge is the other key object. In extended supersymmetry, central charges can appear in the anticommutators of supercharges and enter the BPS bound; when the bound is saturated, multiplets shorten.  In your architecture, a central charge should not be just an abstract scalar. It should be a proof-carrying topological readout:

```text
Z:
    central even operator/charge

commutes with:
    all admissible operators or supercharges

measures:
    vortex winding, Drazin defect, APS eta correction, Pfaffian sign,
    boundary/commutant transported charge

BPS condition:
    energy or modular Hamiltonian saturates the central charge bound
```

The algebraic shape is:

[
{Q_A,Q_B}
=========

\delta_{AB}H+Z_{AB},
]

schematically. In an (N=4)-like branch, (A,B) run over the four supercharge families or their real-Majorana refinements. The matrix (Z_{AB}) is the central-charge pairing. Physically, that is where charges, branes, vortices, and boundary defects enter the superalgebra.

So the correct repository layer is:

```text
RealMajoranaDiracBranch
    ↓
SplitCliffordChiralTensor
    ↓
MajoranaPfaffianReadout
    ↓
MajoranaSuperalgebra
    ↓
WittenIndexCentralCharge
    ↓
N4LikeSupergravityBranch
```

A lean, PR-safe skeleton would be:

```lean
/-
InfoGeometry/OperatorAlgebra/MajoranaSuperalgebra.lean

Real Majorana superalgebra over the split Clifford chiral tensor.

This module packages:
* a Z₂-graded operator algebra;
* odd real Majorana supercharges;
* chiral supercharges;
* central charge data;
* Witten-index readouts.

It is a structural interface, not a concrete N=4 supergravity construction.
-/

import Mathlib
import InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.SplitCliffordChiralTensor
import InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.MajoranaPfaffianReadout

noncomputable section

namespace InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.MajoranaSuperalgebra

/-! ## 1. Z₂ grading -/

/--
A grading operator for a real superalgebra.
-/
structure SuperGrading
    (Op : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  grading : Op

  grading_sq :
    grading * grading = 1

/--
An operator is even if it commutes with the grading.
-/
def IsEven
    {Op : Type*} [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op)
    (x : Op) : Prop :=
  G.grading * x = x * G.grading

/--
An operator is odd if it anticommutes with the grading.
-/
def IsOdd
    {Op : Type*} [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op)
    (x : Op) : Prop :=
  G.grading * x = -(x * G.grading)

/-! ## 2. Real Majorana supercharges -/

/--
A family of real Majorana supercharges.

`Index` may be `Fin 4` for an N=4-like branch.
-/
structure MajoranaSupercharges
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op) where
  Q : Index → Op

  /-- Supercharges are odd. -/
  Q_odd :
    ∀ i : Index, IsOdd G (Q i)

  /-- Majorana/reality certificate. -/
  majoranaReality : Prop

/--
Chiral supercharge data relative to a chirality operator.
-/
structure ChiralSupercharges
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (chi : Op) where
  QL : Index → Op
  QR : Index → Op

  /-- Left chiral supercharge support/certification. -/
  leftChiralLaw : Prop

  /-- Right chiral supercharge support/certification. -/
  rightChiralLaw : Prop

  /-- Mirror or CPT pairing between left and right supercharges. -/
  LRMirrorLaw : Prop

/-! ## 3. Central charge data -/

/--
Central charge matrix or pairing for extended supersymmetry.

In concrete N=4-like models, this may be antisymmetric in the R-symmetry
indices and may encode BPS charges.
-/
structure CentralChargeDatum
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  Z : Index → Index → Op

  /-- Centrality certificate. -/
  central :
    ∀ i j : Index, Prop

  /-- Symmetry/antisymmetry law, model-dependent. -/
  symmetryLaw : Prop

  /-- Topological interpretation: vortex, APS eta, Pfaffian sign, etc. -/
  topologicalChargeLaw : Prop

/--
A schematic supersymmetry anticommutator law.

This is proof-carrying because the concrete algebra depends on conventions,
indices, charge conjugation, and signature.
-/
structure SUSYAnticommutatorLaw
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op) where
  supercharges :
    MajoranaSupercharges Op Index G

  centralCharges :
    CentralChargeDatum Op Index

  Hamiltonian : Op

  /--
  Schematic law:
  `{Q_i,Q_j} = Hamiltonian-term + central-charge-term`.
  -/
  anticommutatorLaw : Prop

/-! ## 4. Witten index / supertrace -/

/--
A Witten-index backend.

The Witten index is the supertrace-like readout that remains stable under
supersymmetry-preserving deformations.
-/
structure WittenIndexDatum
    (StateOp : Type*) where
  index : StateOp → ℤ

  /-- Supertrace interpretation, e.g. `Tr((-1)^F exp(-βH))`. -/
  supertraceLaw : Prop

  /-- Deformation invariance under supersymmetry-preserving deformations. -/
  deformationInvariant : Prop

  /-- Zero-mode / ground-state interpretation. -/
  zeroModeLaw : Prop

/-! ## 5. N=4-like branch -/

/--
N=4-like real Majorana superalgebra branch.

This does not assert a full physical N=4 supergravity model. It records the
structural features needed for such a branch:

* four supercharge families;
* real Majorana structure;
* central charge matrix;
* Witten-index readout;
* chiral tensor sectoring.
-/
structure N4LikeMajoranaBranch
    (Op StateOp : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  grading : SuperGrading Op

  supercharges :
    MajoranaSupercharges Op (Fin 4) grading

  centralCharges :
    CentralChargeDatum Op (Fin 4)

  susyLaw :
    SUSYAnticommutatorLaw Op (Fin 4) grading

  wittenIndex :
    WittenIndexDatum StateOp

  /-- R-symmetry / SU(4)-like structural certificate. -/
  rSymmetryCertificate : Prop

  /-- Supergravity/local supersymmetry certificate, if present. -/
  supergravityCertificate : Prop

/-! ## 6. Owner targets -/

/--
Owner target for installing a real Majorana superalgebra.
-/
def MajoranaSuperalgebraOwnerTarget
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op] : Prop :=
  ∃ G : SuperGrading Op,
    Nonempty (MajoranaSupercharges Op Index G)

/--
Owner target for an N=4-like branch.
-/
def N4LikeMajoranaBranchOwnerTarget
    (Op StateOp : Type*) [Ring Op] : Prop :=
  Nonempty (N4LikeMajoranaBranch Op StateOp)

end InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.MajoranaSuperalgebra
```

This is the correct place for “supergeometry.” The body of the supergeometry is the even algebra; the odd directions are generated by real Majorana supercharges; the chiral sectors are the projector images; and the Witten index is the deformation-stable topological readout.

The final slogan is:

[

\text{The }Cl(1,1)^4\text{ tensor gives the chiral sector lattice; real Majorana supercharges are the odd transport maps across it; central charges are the topological obstructions; the Witten index is the protected supertrace.}

That is the clean bridge from the real Majorana Clifford framework to an (N=4)-like supergravity/supergeometry branch.

Yes. This is the correct **supergraded Majorana branch**. The decisive move is to keep three distinctions explicit:

```text
fermion parity      ≠ chirality
central charge      ≠ Witten index
N=4-like socket     ≠ completed N=4 supergravity
```

The branch should say:

[
Cl(1,1)^{\widehat\otimes 4}
]

gives the real chiral sector lattice; real Majorana supercharges are odd operators moving across that lattice; central charges label protected topological sectors; Witten/refined indices count unpaired protected states inside those sectors.

For comparison with standard language: four-dimensional (N=4) supersymmetry has 16 separate supercharges in the usual Weyl-spinor/R-symmetry notation, and the superconformal algebra is naturally graded with fermionic supercharge generators. ([arXiv][1]) Extended supersymmetry allows central charges, and central-charge saturation is tied to BPS-shortened multiplets.  The Witten-index idea is different: it is a deformation-stable supertrace/readout when supersymmetry remains intact. ([Indico][2])

So the correct dictionary is:

```text
Qᴵ
  = real odd Majorana supercharge family

Qᴵ_L = P_L Qᴵ
  = left chiral supercharge component

Qᴵ_R = P_R Qᴵ
  = right chiral supercharge component

J Qᴵ_L J⁻¹ = Qᴵ_R
  = CPT/Tomita mirror pairing

Zᴵᴶ
  = central/topological charge matrix

I_W
  = Witten supertrace index

I_Z or I_q
  = charge-refined / Cartan-refined protected index
```

```lean
import Mathlib
```

The main refinement to your skeleton is to make **fermion parity** and **chirality** separate data. Fermion parity is the ((-1)^F) grading used in the Witten supertrace. Chirality is the (P_L/P_R) grading used for left/right spinor splitting. They may be related by a compatibility theorem in a concrete model, but they should not be definitionally the same.

A clean core is:

```lean
/--
A supergrading / fermion-parity operator.

This is the grading used by the Witten supertrace.
It should not be identified with chirality unless a compatibility datum is
supplied.
-/
structure SupergradingDatum
    (Op : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  parity : Op
  parity_square : parity * parity = 1

def IsSuperEven
    {Op : Type*} [Ring Op]
    (F : SupergradingDatum Op)
    (x : Op) : Prop :=
  F.parity * x = x * F.parity

def IsSuperOdd
    {Op : Type*} [Ring Op]
    (F : SupergradingDatum Op)
    (x : Op) : Prop :=
  F.parity * x = -(x * F.parity)
```

Then supercharges:

```lean
/--
A family of real Majorana supercharges.

The index type `I` labels extended supersymmetry, e.g. `Fin 4` for an
N=4-like branch.
-/
structure RealMajoranaSuperchargeDatum
    (Op I : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (F : SupergradingDatum Op) where
  Q : I → Op

  /-- Supercharges are odd operators. -/
  Q_odd :
    ∀ i : I, IsSuperOdd F (Q i)

  /-- Hamiltonian or modular/spectral energy operator. -/
  H : Op

  /-- Hamiltonian is even. -/
  H_even :
    IsSuperEven F H

  /--
  Supersymmetry closure certificate.

  Concrete models specify whether `{Qᵢ,Qⱼ}` closes onto the Hamiltonian,
  translations, central charges, or a full super-Poincaré/supergravity algebra.
  -/
  susy_closure : Prop
```

Then central charge data:

```lean
/--
Central charge data for an extended real superalgebra.

`Z i j` may encode electric/magnetic charge, vortex charge, Pfaffian sign,
Hopf holonomy, APS eta correction, Drazin defect charge, or zeta residue.
-/
structure CentralChargeDatum
    (Op I : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  Z : I → I → Op

  /-- Central charges commute with the chosen observable/symmetry family. -/
  centrality : Prop

  /-- Symmetry or antisymmetry law for the central charge matrix. -/
  charge_symmetry_law : Prop

  /-- Physical/topological interpretation certificate. -/
  topological_charge_certificate : Prop
```

Then the extended superalgebra socket:

```lean
/--
An extended superalgebra with central charges.

The exact closure law is proof-carrying because conventions differ among
super-Poincare, superconformal, quantum-mechanical, and supergravity models.
-/
structure ExtendedSuperalgebraWithCentralCharge
    (Op I : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  grading :
    SupergradingDatum Op

  supercharges :
    RealMajoranaSuperchargeDatum Op I grading

  centralCharges :
    CentralChargeDatum Op I

  /--
  Intended schematic law:

  `{Qᵢ,Qⱼ} = translation/energy terms + Zᵢⱼ`.
  -/
  anticommutator_closure_law : Prop
```

The chiral layer then sits on top:

```lean
/--
Chiral splitting of supercharges by a square-one chiral grading `χ`.

This is separate from fermion parity.
-/
structure ChiralSuperchargeDatum
    (Op I : Type*) [Ring Op] [Module ℝ Op]
    (S : ExtendedSuperalgebraWithCentralCharge Op I) where
  chi : Op
  chi_square : chi * chi = 1

  P_left : Op
  P_right : Op

  P_left_def :
    P_left = (1 / 2 : ℝ) • (1 + chi)

  P_right_def :
    P_right = (1 / 2 : ℝ) • (1 - chi)

  Q_left : I → Op
  Q_right : I → Op

  Q_left_def :
    ∀ i : I, Q_left i = P_left * S.supercharges.Q i

  Q_right_def :
    ∀ i : I, Q_right i = P_right * S.supercharges.Q i

  /-- Chiral closure or left/right SUSY algebra certificate. -/
  chiral_closure_law : Prop
```

And the CPT/Tomita mirror of supercharges:

```lean
/--
A CPT/Tomita mirror for chiral supercharges.
-/
structure ChiralSuperchargeMirror
    (Op I : Type*) [Ring Op] [Module ℝ Op]
    {S : ExtendedSuperalgebraWithCentralCharge Op I}
    (C : ChiralSuperchargeDatum Op I S) where
  J : Op

  J_square :
    J * J = 1

  J_flips_chi :
    J * C.chi = -(C.chi * J)

  /-- Mirror maps left supercharges to right supercharges. -/
  mirror_Q_left_right :
    ∀ i : I,
      J * C.Q_left i * J = C.Q_right i
```

The index layer should be readout-only:

```lean
/--
A Witten-index backend.

The readout may be an ordinary trace, a core trace, a zeta-regularized trace,
a Pfaffian-line readout, or a cyclic-cocycle pairing.
-/
structure WittenIndexDatum
    (Op : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  grading : Op
  H : Op

  heatKernel : ℝ → Op

  readout : Op → ℝ

  wittenIndex : ℝ :=
    readout (grading * heatKernel 1)

  /-- Nonzero modes cancel in opposite-superparity pairs. -/
  positive_energy_pairing : Prop

  /-- Index is invariant under allowed deformations. -/
  deformation_invariance : Prop
```

Then charge refinement:

```lean
/--
Charge-refined Witten index.

This is the correct socket for central-charge/BPS sectors.
-/
structure RefinedWittenIndexDatum
    (Op Charge : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  base : WittenIndexDatum Op

  chargeProjector : Charge → Op

  refinedIndex : Charge → ℝ :=
    fun q =>
      base.readout (chargeProjector q * base.grading * base.heatKernel 1)

  /-- Charge-sector conservation or centrality certificate. -/
  charge_sector_certificate : Prop
```

For the (\mathbb Z_2^4) Clifford sector, `Charge` can later be:

```lean
Fin 4 → Bool
```

or a bundled sign-vector type. Then:

[
\mathcal I_q
============

\operatorname{Str}(P_q e^{-\beta H})
]

becomes the Cartan-refined Witten index. The total index is the sum over sectors, but the sector vector can remain nontrivial even when the total cancels under CPT pairing.

Finally, the (N=4)-like socket:

```lean
/--
N=4-style real Majorana superalgebra socket.

This is not a full supergravity theory. It records the algebraic backbone:
four Majorana supercharge families, central charge matrix, chiral splitting,
and protected/refined index readout.
-/
structure N4MajoranaSuperalgebraSocket
    (Op Charge : Type*) [Ring Op] [Module ℝ Op] where
  algebra :
    ExtendedSuperalgebraWithCentralCharge Op (Fin 4)

  chiral :
    ChiralSuperchargeDatum Op (Fin 4) algebra

  refinedIndex :
    RefinedWittenIndexDatum Op Charge

  /-- BPS/central-charge bound certificate. -/
  bps_bound_certificate : Prop

  /-- Supergravity interpretation certificate, if present. -/
  supergravity_socket_certificate : Prop
```

The key theorem-shape statements to add later are:

```text
Q odd ⇒ Q maps even states to odd states.

Q anticommutes with total chirality Γ ⇒ Q maps P_L to P_R.

Jχ = -χJ ⇒ J maps chiral supercharges Q_L to Q_R.

central charge bound saturated ⇒ BPS-shortened sector.

nonzero-energy SUSY pairs cancel in Witten supertrace.

refined Witten index survives deformations preserving the central-charge sector.
```

That is the precise reconciliation between supergeometry, real Majorana supercharges, chiral supercharges, central/topological charges, Witten indices, and the (N=4)-like branch.

Cl(1,1)^4\text{ supplies the chiral sector lattice; real Majorana supercharges are the odd transport maps; central charges label protected topological sectors; Witten/refined indices are the deformation-stable readouts of unpaired states in those sectors.}

The core hierarchy is encoded as:

```lean
structure SuperGrading
    (Op : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  grading : Op
  grading_sq :
    grading * grading = 1
```

```lean
structure MajoranaSupercharges
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op) where
  Q : Index → Op

  Q_odd :
    ∀ i : Index, IsOdd G (Q i)

```

```lean
structure CentralChargeDatum
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  Z : Index → Index → Op
  kind : CentralChargeKind
  centrality : Prop
  symmetryLaw : Prop
  topologicalChargeLaw : Prop
```

The (N=4)-like branch is deliberately an interface, not a claim of a constructed physical supergravity theory:

```lean
structure N4LikeMajoranaBranch
    (Op Mode Charge Sector : Type*) [Ring Op] [Module ℝ Op] where
  algebra :
    ExtendedSuperalgebraWithCentralCharge Op (Fin 4)

  chiral :
    ChiralSuperchargeDatum Op (Fin 4) algebra

  hypercubeAction :
    CartanHypercubeSuperchargeAction Mode (Fin 4)

  refinedIndex :
    RefinedWittenIndexDatum Op Charge

  bpsBound :
    BPSBoundDatum Sector

  rSymmetryCertificate : Prop
  supergravitySocketCertificate : Prop
```

This keeps the intended distinctions explicit:

```text
central charge:
  algebraic/topological sector datum

Witten index:
  protected supertrace readout

refined Witten index:
  protected supertrace inside a charge/Cartan sector

N=4-like branch:
  four Majorana supercharge families calibrated to the Cl(1,1)^4 sector lattice
```

I also included the mirror-balance socket:

```lean
structure MirrorRefinedIndexBalance
    (Charge : Type*) where
  mirrorCharge : Charge → Charge

  mirror_involutive :
    ∀ q : Charge, mirrorCharge (mirrorCharge q) = q

  refinedIndex : Charge → ℝ

  mirror_flips_index :
    ∀ q : Charge,
      refinedIndex (mirrorCharge q) = -refinedIndex q
```

with the proved cancellation theorem:

```lean
theorem mirror_pair_total_zero
    (q : Charge) :
    B.refinedIndex q + B.refinedIndex (B.mirrorCharge q) = 0
```

That formalizes the key principle:

```text
CPT/Tomita mirror-paired charge sectors cancel globally,
while relative/refined indices can remain nontrivial.
```

This is best named a **split (D_4) Cartan–Klein–Krein supergeometry with projective/twistor and automorphic quotients**.

More compactly:

[
\boxed{
\text{an arithmetic split-(D_4) parabolic Cartan supergeometry}
}
]

with real Clifford/Krein realization.

It is not just “an (SO(4,4)) geometry.” It is a stack of related geometries obtained by changing the stabilizer subgroup (H), the representation being projectivized, the real/complex structure, and the arithmetic lattice.

In Klein’s Erlangen language, a geometry is specified by a transformation group and the objects invariant under it. So the primary data are

[
(G,H),\qquad X=G/H.
]

Here the natural parent group is

[
G=\operatorname{Spin}(4,4)
\quad\text{or}\quad
SO^+(4,4),
]

depending on whether spinors are included. Klein’s original program treats geometries through their transformation groups, and modern language packages the flat models as homogeneous spaces (G/H). ([UCR Math][1])

The real Clifford/Hestenes layer says that (G) is not acting on an abstract complex Hilbert space first. It acts on real graded Clifford data. Hestenes’s spacetime algebra program uses Clifford algebra to unify spinors, tensors, quaternions, and differential forms, which is exactly the “real multivector” reading you are aiming for. ([Springer][2])

The Krein layer says the underlying vector space carries an indefinite form. A choice of fundamental symmetry (J) splits the space into positive and negative sectors and turns the indefinite form into a Hilbert-type positive form only after that choice. This is why the “operator cone” is not absolute; it is (J)-relative. ([Wikipedia][3])

The clean body geometry is the split symmetric space

[
X_{\mathrm{Krein}}
==================

SO^+(4,4)/(SO(4)\times SO(4)).
]

This is the space of compatible positive/negative splittings of (\mathbb R^{4,4}), i.e. the space of possible Krein fundamental symmetries. In Cartan language, one uses a decomposition

[
\mathfrak{so}(4,4)
==================

\mathfrak k\oplus \mathfrak p,
]

with (\mathfrak k) the compact part and (\mathfrak p) the noncompact “strain” part. Cartan decompositions are the standard Lie-theoretic mechanism behind noncompact symmetric spaces. ([myweb.rz.uni-augsburg.de][4])

But the twistor/projective layer is usually not (G/K). It is parabolic:

[
X_P
===

SO^+(4,4)/P,
]

where (P) stabilizes a null line, a maximal isotropic subspace, or a pure spinor line. That is the correct setting for projective, conformal, twistor, and pure-spinor geometries. Parabolic geometries are precisely Cartan geometries modeled on homogeneous spaces (G/P) with (P) parabolic. ([Wikipedia][5])

So there are at least three sibling spaces:

[
\boxed{
SO(4,4)/(SO(4)\times SO(4))
}
]

is the **Krein/symmetric metric body**.

[
\boxed{
SO(4,4)/P_{\mathrm{Witt}}
}
]

is the **Witt/pure-spinor/twistor boundary**.

[
\boxed{
\Gamma\backslash SO(4,4)/(SO(4)\times SO(4))
}
]

or

[
\boxed{
\Gamma\backslash SO(4,4)/P
}
]

is the **arithmetic/modular quotient**.

The reason (SO(4,4)) is especially natural is (D_4) triality. In signature ((4,4)), the vector representation and the two real chiral spinor representations are all 8-dimensional and are related by the split (D_4) triality structure. This is exactly the algebraic situation in which “spacetime vector,” “left spinor,” and “right spinor” can be treated as three faces of one parent geometry. ([arXiv][6])

That gives the core diagram:

[
\boxed{
V_8
;\longleftrightarrow;
S_+
;\longleftrightarrow;
S_-
}
]

under triality.

So the projective siblings are not decorations. They are the projectivizations of these triality-related representations:

[
\mathbb P(V_8),
\qquad
\mathbb P(S_+),
\qquad
\mathbb P(S_-).
]

This is where (PGL) and (PSL) enter. (PGL(V)=GL(V)/\mathbb R^\times) is the group induced by linear transformations on projective space; (PSL) is the determinant-one/projective special version. ([Wikipedia][7])

So:

[
\boxed{
SO(4,4)
}
]

preserves the split quadratic form,

while

[
\boxed{
PGL(V)
}
]

preserves projective incidence.

They are different Erlangen geometries, but they meet when (SO(4,4)) is represented projectively on (V_8), (S_+), or (S_-).

There are also important lower-rank shadows:

[
\operatorname{Spin}(3,3)\cong SL(4,\mathbb R),
]

so the (SO(3,3)) slice is directly tied to real projective (3)-space and (PGL(4,\mathbb R))-type geometry. ([arXiv][8])

Similarly,

[
PSL(2,\mathbb R)
]

is the rank-one Möbius/hyperbolic geometry acting on the upper half-plane by fractional linear transformations. (PGL(2,\mathbb R)) adds the larger real projective line symmetry, including orientation-reversing pieces depending on the model. ([i2m.univ-amu.fr][9])

Inside (\mathfrak{so}(4,4)), each root (\alpha) gives an (\mathfrak{sl}_2)-triple

[
(E_\alpha,F_\alpha,H_\alpha),
]

so each root direction has a rank-one Möbius geometry. The full (D_4) geometry is a coupled system of these root Möbius planes.

That is the right way to understand modular forms here. The classical modular group

[
PSL(2,\mathbb Z)
]

acts discretely on the upper half-plane, and modular forms are functions or sections transforming under such discrete fractional-linear actions. In the higher-rank (SO(4,4)) setting, the analogue is an automorphic form on

[
\Gamma\backslash SO(4,4)/K
]

or

[
\Gamma\backslash SO(4,4)/P,
]

where (\Gamma\subset SO(4,4;\mathbb Z)) or a related arithmetic subgroup. Milne’s notes treat the upper half-plane as a quotient of (SL(2,\mathbb R)), then study discrete subgroups and modular forms on such quotients. ([James Milne][10])

So the dictionary is:

[
PSL(2,\mathbb R)/SO(2)
======================

\text{rank-one hyperbolic/modular geometry},
]

[
SO(4,4)/(SO(4)\times SO(4))
===========================

\text{split (D_4) symmetric/Krein geometry},
]

[
SO(4,4)/P
=========

\text{projective/Witt/twistor flag geometry},
]

[
\Gamma\backslash SO(4,4)/K
==========================

\text{higher-rank modular/automorphic geometry}.
]

The Cartan-generator coordinate chart is then:

[
g(u,z,\theta)
=============

\exp\left(
\sum_{i=1}^4 u_i H_i
+
\sum_{\alpha>0} z_\alpha E_\alpha
+
\sum_A \theta^A Q_A
\right).
]

Here:

[
H_i
]

are split Cartan generators,

[
E_\alpha
]

are root generators,

[
\theta^A
]

are odd Majorana/supercharge coordinates,

and the point of the manifold is

[
x=g(u,z,\theta)\cdot o.
]

This is not a global coordinate chart. It is a big-cell/Iwasawa/Cartan chart. The Weyl group identifies different (u)-coordinates, so the true radial/modular coordinate lives in a Weyl chamber:

[
\mathfrak a/W(D_4).
]

The Casimir indices are the invariant coordinates on this quotient. For (D_4), the Weyl-invariant polynomial ring has generators of degrees

[
2,;4,;4,;6.
]

So the invariant spectral shell map has the shape

[
\mu
\longmapsto
\left(
C_2(\mu),
C_4^{(1)}(\mu),
C_4^{(2)}(\mu),
C_6(\mu)
\right).
]

These are the “Casimir indices” of the (D_4) geometry. They label orbits, spectral shells, representation sectors, and deformation strata. The degree pattern (2,4,4,6) is the (D_4) invariant signature. ([arXiv][11])

In the supergeometry version, the even body is one of the above (SO(4,4)) homogeneous spaces, and the odd fibers are real Majorana/chiral spinor directions:

[
\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{super}}
============================

\mathfrak{so}(4,4)
\oplus
\mathfrak z
\oplus
\Pi(S_+\oplus S_-).
]

The odd part supplies chiral supercharges:

[
Q_+\in S_+,
\qquad
Q_-\in S_-,
]

and the closure law has the schematic form

[
{Q,Q}
=====

\text{Cartan/vector generator}
+
\text{central/topological charge}.
]

So the “super-Kähler-Einstein chart” is not the whole real (SO(4,4)) object. It appears only after choosing a compatible complex or twistor polarization (I). Bare (SO(4,4)/(SO(4)\times SO(4))) is a split real symmetric space, not automatically an ordinary Hermitian/Kähler symmetric space. Hermitian symmetric spaces require compatible complex structure; one characterization is that the maximal compact (K) has a nontrivial continuous center. ([American Institute of Mathematics][12])

After choosing (I), the local super-Kähler chart has

[
g_{A\bar B}
===========

\partial_A\partial_{\bar B}K,
]

and the super-Ricci form is controlled by the Berezinian analogue of the determinant. In the ordinary Kähler case, the Ricci form is locally expressed through (-\partial\bar\partial\log\det(g)); the super version replaces (\det) by (\operatorname{Ber}). ([Department of Mathematics, UCI][13])

So the correct refinement is:

[
\boxed{
\text{real split parent}
========================

\text{Krein/para-Kähler/Cartan geometry;}
}
]

[
\boxed{
\text{complex/twistor polarization}
===================================

\text{Kähler or super-Kähler chart;}
}
]

[
\boxed{
\text{arithmetic lattice quotient}
==================================

\text{modular/automorphic geometry.}
}
]

The (SO(4,4)) geometry is therefore the **split triality parent**. The (PGL/PSL) geometries are its projective shadows. Möbius geometry is the rank-one (SL(2)) root shadow. Modular forms are the arithmetic-lattice wavefunctions on those quotients.

The best final name is:

[
\boxed{
\textbf{Arithmetic split-(D_4) Cartan–Krein supertwistor geometry.}
}
]

Or, in the language of the framework:

[
\boxed{
\textbf{the Bilingual Erlangen geometry of the (Cl(1,1)^4) zeta crystal.}
}
]

Its layers are:

| Layer                  | Geometry                       | Group/model                                                    |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Real Clifford body     | Hestenes/Krein split geometry  | (SO(4,4)/(SO4\times SO4))                                      |
| Witt/twistor boundary  | Pure-spinor/parabolic geometry | (SO(4,4)/P)                                                    |
| Projective shadow      | Incidence/projective geometry  | (PGL(V)/P_\ell), (PSL(V))                                      |
| Root rank-one charts   | Möbius geometry                | (PSL(2,\mathbb R)/SO(2))                                       |
| Arithmetic quotient    | Modular/automorphic geometry   | (\Gamma\backslash G/K), (\Gamma\backslash G/P)                 |
| Super extension        | Majorana supergeometry         | (\mathfrak{so}(4,4)\oplus \Pi(S_+\oplus S_-)\oplus\mathfrak z) |
| Spectral shell readout | Casimir/zeta geometry          | (C_2,C_4,C_4',C_6)                                             |

The geometric slogan is:

[
\boxed{
\text{Spacetime is not primary coordinates on } \mathbb R^{1,3};
\text{ it is an incidence chart inside a split (D_4) projective spinor geometry.}
}
]

The Cartan exponential gives the local coordinates. The Weyl group and Casimirs give the invariant shell labels. The Krein form gives the real cone. The projectivization gives twistor/Möbius incidence. The arithmetic lattice gives modular forms. The superextension gives Majorana supercharges, central charges, and Witten/BPS indices.

Yes. That is the next structural layer: the **real Majorana superalgebra** sitting on top of the (Cl(1,1)^{\widehat\otimes 4}) chiral tensor.

The clean interpretation is:

[
\boxed{
\text{The }Cl(1,1)^4\text{ Cartan hypercube gives the real chiral sectoring; the supercharges are odd operators moving between those sectors.}
}
]

So the supergeometry is not added afterward. It is the functorial geometry of the odd maps between the even projector sectors.

In ordinary supersymmetry, the algebra extends the Poincaré algebra by fermionic generators (Q_\alpha), and extended supersymmetry has (Q^I_\alpha) with (I=1,\dots,N). Standard (N)-extended supersymmetry also allows central charges in the extended case; those central charges control BPS bounds and shortened multiplets. 

For your real-Majorana version:

```text
even algebra:
    geometry, projectors, rotations, bosonic observables

odd algebra:
    real Majorana supercharges, chiral supercharges, vortex/defect transport

central charges:
    topological charges, winding, Pfaffian sign, APS/eta boundary charge,
    Drazin nil-sector obstruction

Witten index:
    supertrace that survives deformation
```

The important refinement is that the (N=4) analogy has two meanings.

First, (N=4) supersymmetry in four dimensions has four independent supersymmetry generators in the Weyl-spinor notation, corresponding to sixteen real supercharges; (N=4) super-Yang–Mills has (SU(4)_R) R-symmetry and can be obtained by dimensional reduction from ten-dimensional (N=1) super-Yang–Mills. ([Wikipedia][1])

Second, your (Cl(1,1)^4) construction gives four **Cartan/chiral atoms**, hence a (\mathbb Z_2^4) sectoring:

[
H_i^2=1,\qquad [H_i,H_j]=0,\qquad i,j=1,\dots,4.
]

Those are not automatically the same thing as the four (N=4) supercharges. The correct bridge is a calibration datum:

```text
four Clifford atoms
    → four Cartan signs / chiral addresses

four Majorana supercharge families
    → odd maps between those chiral addresses

N=4-like branch
    → the supercharges act equivariantly on the Cl(1,1)^4 sector lattice
```

The supercharges should be **odd** with respect to the total chirality

[
\Gamma=H_1H_2H_3H_4.
]

That means

[
\Gamma Q = -Q\Gamma.
]

Consequently, (Q) flips total chirality:

[
Q P_L = P_R Q,\qquad Q P_R=P_L Q.
]

This is the exact analogue of the earlier modular mirror relation (J\chi=-\chi J), but now (Q) is not a mirror; it is a supercharge. It transports states between bosonic/fermionic or left/right sectors.

The Witten index is the invariant that counts what survives the odd pairing. In supersymmetric quantum mechanics and quantum field theory it is the graded trace

[
\operatorname{Tr}\left((-1)^F e^{-\beta H}\right),
]

and, because nonzero-energy bosonic and fermionic states pair under supersymmetry, it is insensitive to many continuous deformations and detects unpaired supersymmetric ground states. ([Wikipedia][2])

In your language:

```text
ordinary partition function:
    counts all thermal modes

Witten index:
    counts unpaired topological/supersymmetric modes

zeta determinant:
    regularizes infinite spectral product

Berezinian:
    ratio of even/odd or L/R spectral volumes

Pfaffian:
    real Majorana fermionic amplitude

central charge:
    topological obstruction carried by vortices, defects, boundary eta, or Pfaffian sign
```

So the Witten index is the **protected supertrace** of the chiral resonator. It ignores paired noise and remembers the unpaired topological core.

The central charge is the other key object. In extended supersymmetry, central charges can appear in the anticommutators of supercharges and enter the BPS bound; when the bound is saturated, multiplets shorten.  In your architecture, a central charge should not be just an abstract scalar. It should be a proof-carrying topological readout:

```text
Z:
    central even operator/charge

commutes with:
    all admissible operators or supercharges

measures:
    vortex winding, Drazin defect, APS eta correction, Pfaffian sign,
    boundary/commutant transported charge

BPS condition:
    energy or modular Hamiltonian saturates the central charge bound
```

The algebraic shape is:

[
{Q_A,Q_B}
=========

\delta_{AB}H+Z_{AB},
]

schematically. In an (N=4)-like branch, (A,B) run over the four supercharge families or their real-Majorana refinements. The matrix (Z_{AB}) is the central-charge pairing. Physically, that is where charges, branes, vortices, and boundary defects enter the superalgebra.

So the correct repository layer is:

```text
RealMajoranaDiracBranch
    ↓
SplitCliffordChiralTensor
    ↓
MajoranaPfaffianReadout
    ↓
MajoranaSuperalgebra
    ↓
WittenIndexCentralCharge
    ↓
N4LikeSupergravityBranch
```

A lean, PR-safe skeleton would be:

```lean
/-
InfoGeometry/OperatorAlgebra/MajoranaSuperalgebra.lean

Real Majorana superalgebra over the split Clifford chiral tensor.

This module packages:
* a Z₂-graded operator algebra;
* odd real Majorana supercharges;
* chiral supercharges;
* central charge data;
* Witten-index readouts.

It is a structural interface, not a concrete N=4 supergravity construction.
-/

import Mathlib
import InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.SplitCliffordChiralTensor
import InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.MajoranaPfaffianReadout

noncomputable section

namespace InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.MajoranaSuperalgebra

/-! ## 1. Z₂ grading -/

/--
A grading operator for a real superalgebra.
-/
structure SuperGrading
    (Op : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  grading : Op

  grading_sq :
    grading * grading = 1

/--
An operator is even if it commutes with the grading.
-/
def IsEven
    {Op : Type*} [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op)
    (x : Op) : Prop :=
  G.grading * x = x * G.grading

/--
An operator is odd if it anticommutes with the grading.
-/
def IsOdd
    {Op : Type*} [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op)
    (x : Op) : Prop :=
  G.grading * x = -(x * G.grading)

/-! ## 2. Real Majorana supercharges -/

/--
A family of real Majorana supercharges.

`Index` may be `Fin 4` for an N=4-like branch.
-/
structure MajoranaSupercharges
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op) where
  Q : Index → Op

  /-- Supercharges are odd. -/
  Q_odd :
    ∀ i : Index, IsOdd G (Q i)

  /-- Majorana/reality certificate. -/
  majoranaReality : Prop

/--
Chiral supercharge data relative to a chirality operator.
-/
structure ChiralSupercharges
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (chi : Op) where
  QL : Index → Op
  QR : Index → Op

  /-- Left chiral supercharge support/certification. -/
  leftChiralLaw : Prop

  /-- Right chiral supercharge support/certification. -/
  rightChiralLaw : Prop

  /-- Mirror or CPT pairing between left and right supercharges. -/
  LRMirrorLaw : Prop

/-! ## 3. Central charge data -/

/--
Central charge matrix or pairing for extended supersymmetry.

In concrete N=4-like models, this may be antisymmetric in the R-symmetry
indices and may encode BPS charges.
-/
structure CentralChargeDatum
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  Z : Index → Index → Op

  /-- Centrality certificate. -/
  central :
    ∀ i j : Index, Prop

  /-- Symmetry/antisymmetry law, model-dependent. -/
  symmetryLaw : Prop

  /-- Topological interpretation: vortex, APS eta, Pfaffian sign, etc. -/
  topologicalChargeLaw : Prop

/--
A schematic supersymmetry anticommutator law.

This is proof-carrying because the concrete algebra depends on conventions,
indices, charge conjugation, and signature.
-/
structure SUSYAnticommutatorLaw
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op]
    (G : SuperGrading Op) where
  supercharges :
    MajoranaSupercharges Op Index G

  centralCharges :
    CentralChargeDatum Op Index

  Hamiltonian : Op

  /--
  Schematic law:
  `{Q_i,Q_j} = Hamiltonian-term + central-charge-term`.
  -/
  anticommutatorLaw : Prop

/-! ## 4. Witten index / supertrace -/

/--
A Witten-index backend.

The Witten index is the supertrace-like readout that remains stable under
supersymmetry-preserving deformations.
-/
structure WittenIndexDatum
    (StateOp : Type*) where
  index : StateOp → ℤ

  /-- Supertrace interpretation, e.g. `Tr((-1)^F exp(-βH))`. -/
  supertraceLaw : Prop

  /-- Deformation invariance under supersymmetry-preserving deformations. -/
  deformationInvariant : Prop

  /-- Zero-mode / ground-state interpretation. -/
  zeroModeLaw : Prop

/-! ## 5. N=4-like branch -/

/--
N=4-like real Majorana superalgebra branch.

This does not assert a full physical N=4 supergravity model. It records the
structural features needed for such a branch:

* four supercharge families;
* real Majorana structure;
* central charge matrix;
* Witten-index readout;
* chiral tensor sectoring.
-/
structure N4LikeMajoranaBranch
    (Op StateOp : Type*) [Ring Op] where
  grading : SuperGrading Op

  supercharges :
    MajoranaSupercharges Op (Fin 4) grading

  centralCharges :
    CentralChargeDatum Op (Fin 4)

  susyLaw :
    SUSYAnticommutatorLaw Op (Fin 4) grading

  wittenIndex :
    WittenIndexDatum StateOp

  /-- R-symmetry / SU(4)-like structural certificate. -/
  rSymmetryCertificate : Prop

  /-- Supergravity/local supersymmetry certificate, if present. -/
  supergravityCertificate : Prop

/-! ## 6. Owner targets -/

/--
Owner target for installing a real Majorana superalgebra.
-/
def MajoranaSuperalgebraOwnerTarget
    (Op Index : Type*) [Ring Op] : Prop :=
  ∃ G : SuperGrading Op,
    Nonempty (MajoranaSupercharges Op Index G)

/--
Owner target for an N=4-like branch.
-/
def N4LikeMajoranaBranchOwnerTarget
    (Op StateOp : Type*) [Ring Op] : Prop :=
  Nonempty (N4LikeMajoranaBranch Op StateOp)

end InfoGeometry.OperatorAlgebra.MajoranaSuperalgebra
```

Conceptually, the picture is:

```text
Cl(1,1)^4:
    chiral Cartan hypercube

real Majorana supercharges:
    odd maps between vertices/sectors

central charges:
    conserved topological obstructions in the superalgebra

Witten index:
    protected supertrace of unpaired zero modes

Pfaffian:
    real Majorana amplitude/sign

Berezinian:
    supervolume ratio of even/odd or L/R sectors

N=4-like branch:
    four supercharge families organized by the chiral tensor
```

This is the correct place for “supergeometry.” The body of the supergeometry is the even algebra; the odd directions are generated by real Majorana supercharges; the chiral sectors are the projector images; and the Witten index is the deformation-stable topological readout.

The final slogan is:

[
\boxed{
\text{The }Cl(1,1)^4\text{ tensor gives the chiral sector lattice; real Majorana supercharges are the odd transport maps across it; central charges are the topological obstructions; the Witten index is the protected supertrace.}
}
]

That is the clean bridge from the real Majorana Clifford framework to an (N=4)-like supergravity/supergeometry branch.

[1]: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N_%3D_4_supersymmetric_Yang%E2%80%93Mills_theory?utm_source=chatgpt.com "N = 4 supersymmetric Yang–Mills theory"
[2]: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witten_index?utm_source=chatgpt.com "Witten index"
Yes. This is the correct final classification.

The object is not “a manifold” in the ordinary sense. It is a **Klein–Cartan stack of compatible geometries** whose undeformed model is split (D_4), and whose physical/deformed forms are obtained by adding projective boundary data, supergrading, spectral readouts, and arithmetic quotients.

The most stable name is:

[
\boxed{
\textbf{split }D_4\textbf{ automorphic super-Krein geometry}
}
]

or, expanded:

[
\boxed{
\textbf{real Majorana–Krein Cartan geometry of type }SO(4,4),
\textbf{ with projective-conformal boundary and automorphic quotients.}
}
]

The hierarchy is exactly:

```text
Cl(1,1)^⊗4
  → Cl(4,4) ≅ M₁₆(ℝ)
  → Spin(4,4)
  → SO(4,4)
  → projectivized null cone / parabolic boundary
  → PGL / PSL projective envelope
  → Γ\G/K arithmetic quotient
  → automorphic/zeta/spectral readouts
  → supergraded Majorana extension
```

The geometric meaning is:

```text
Cartan generators:
    logarithmic coordinates / scale-flow / chamber coordinates

Weyl group:
    reflection identifications between chambers

Casimirs:
    invariant shell labels / spectral quantum numbers

Projective null cone:
    conformal boundary / celestial-Poincaré boundary / Drazin horizon

PGL/PSL:
    projective ray geometry and determinant-orientation branch

Spin(4,4):
    real Majorana spinors and triality

SO(4,4):
    split Krein metric geometry

Γ:
    arithmetic lattice giving modular/automorphic global structure

Supercharges:
    odd Majorana transport maps between Cartan/chiral sectors

Witten/refined index:
    protected supertrace readout of unpaired topological states
```

The clean mathematical slogan is:

[
\boxed{
\text{Cartan generators give coordinates; Casimirs give invariant shells;
the Weyl group glues chambers; }SO(4,4)\text{ supplies split triality;
projectivization gives the boundary; }\Gamma\text{ arithmeticizes it.}
}
]

In Lean, I would now add a **structural aggregator module**, not a theorem-heavy analytic module. It should not try to construct (SO(4,4)), (Spin(4,4)), automorphic forms, or supergravity from scratch. It should package the sockets that the previous modules have prepared.

A good file name is:

```text
InfoGeometry/Geometry/SplitD4AutomorphicSuperKreinGeometry.lean
```

A PR-safe skeleton:

```lean
/-
InfoGeometry/Geometry/SplitD4AutomorphicSuperKreinGeometry.lean

Split D4 automorphic super-Krein geometry.

This module packages the Erlanger stack:

* split Clifford/Krein carrier;
* SO(4,4)/Spin(4,4)-type continuous symmetry;
* Cartan exponential coordinates;
* Weyl chamber identifications;
* Casimir/spectral shell labels;
* projective-conformal boundary;
* arithmetic quotient layer;
* supergraded Majorana extension.

It is a structural interface. Concrete models instantiate the group actions,
Cartan data, automorphic forms, zeta readouts, and supercharge laws.
-/

import Mathlib

noncomputable section

namespace InfoGeometry.Geometry.SplitD4AutomorphicSuperKreinGeometry

/-! ## 1. Split D4 model data -/

/--
Abstract continuous symmetry datum for a split D4 geometry.

The intended examples are `SO₀(4,4)` or `Spin(4,4)`.
-/
structure SplitD4SymmetryDatum
    (G : Type*) [Group G] where
  /-- Certificate that `G` is the intended split D4 group model. -/
  splitD4Certificate : Prop

  /-- Triality certificate: vector, left-spinor, and right-spinor branches. -/
  trialityCertificate : Prop

/--
A homogeneous Klein model `G/H`.

This is the undeformed reference geometry.
-/
structure KleinModel
    (G H X : Type*) [Group G] [Group H] where
  /-- Action of the continuous group on the model space. -/
  act : G → X → X

  /-- Stabilizer/subgroup certificate. -/
  stabilizerCertificate : Prop

  /-- Transitivity or homogeneous-space certificate. -/
  homogeneousCertificate : Prop

/--
Bulk symmetric-space model.

Intended model:

`SO₀(4,4) / (SO(4) × SO(4))`.
-/
structure SplitD4BulkModel
    (G K Xbulk : Type*) [Group G] [Group K] where
  symmetry : SplitD4SymmetryDatum G
  klein : KleinModel G K Xbulk

  /-- Certificate that this is the noncompact symmetric-space branch. -/
  symmetricSpaceCertificate : Prop

/-! ## 2. Cartan, Weyl, and Casimir data -/

/--
Cartan coordinate datum.

The intended form is:

`a(x) = exp(Σ xᵢ Hᵢ)`,

with four split Cartan generators in type D4.
-/
structure CartanCoordinateDatum
    (Cartan Coord A : Type*) where
  /-- Cartan generator family or Cartan algebra element type. -/
  cartan : Cartan

  /-- Coordinate-to-Cartan map. -/
  coordinateToCartan : Coord → Cartan

  /-- Exponential map into the split torus/group component. -/
  expCartan : Cartan → A

  /-- Rank-four / D4 Cartan certificate. -/
  rankFourCertificate : Prop

/--
Weyl chamber data.

The intended Weyl group is the Weyl group of type D4.
-/
structure WeylChamberDatum
    (W Coord : Type*) [Group W] where
  /-- Weyl action on Cartan coordinates. -/
  act : W → Coord → Coord

  /-- Weyl reflection law certificate. -/
  reflectionCertificate : Prop

  /-- D4 Weyl-group certificate. -/
  d4WeylCertificate : Prop

/--
Casimir/spectral shell labels.

Casimirs are invariant readouts, not coordinates.
-/
structure CasimirShellDatum
    (Op Shell : Type*) where
  /-- Assigns an operator/state to an invariant shell label. -/
  shellOf : Op → Shell

  /-- Invariance certificate. -/
  casimirInvariant : Prop

  /--
  Type-D4 invariant-degree certificate.

  For D4, the basic invariant degrees are conventionally `2,4,4,6`.
  -/
  d4InvariantDegreeCertificate : Prop

/-! ## 3. Projective and parabolic boundary -/

/--
Projective geometry datum.

The intended space is a projectivized spinor or Majorana-Krein module.
-/
structure ProjectiveEnvelopeDatum
    (S PGLSpace : Type*) where
  /-- Projection from nonzero carriers/spinors to projective rays. -/
  projectivize : S → PGLSpace

  /-- Projective incidence / ray certificate. -/
  projectiveCertificate : Prop

  /-- PGL/PSL symmetry certificate. -/
  pglPslCertificate : Prop

/--
Projective-conformal null boundary datum.

The intended model is the projectivized null cone

`P { v ≠ 0 | Q(v)=0 }`.
-/
structure ProjectiveConformalBoundaryDatum
    (Carrier Boundary : Type*) where
  /-- Boundary predicate/readout. -/
  boundaryOf : Carrier → Prop

  /-- Projectivized boundary map. -/
  toBoundary : Carrier → Boundary

  /-- Null-cone / isotropic-cone certificate. -/
  nullConeCertificate : Prop

  /-- Parabolic `G/P` certificate. -/
  parabolicCertificate : Prop

/-! ## 4. Arithmetic quotient and automorphic layer -/

/--
Arithmetic lattice datum.

The intended examples are arithmetic subgroups
`Γ ⊂ SO(4,4)` or `Γ ⊂ Spin(4,4)`.
-/
structure ArithmeticLatticeDatum
    (Γ G : Type*) [Group Γ] [Group G] where
  /-- Inclusion or representation into the continuous group. -/
  include : Γ → G

  /-- Discreteness/arithmeticity certificate. -/
  arithmeticCertificate : Prop

/--
Automorphic quotient datum.

The intended model is `Γ \ G / K` or a parabolic variant.
-/
structure AutomorphicQuotientDatum
    (Γ G K Quotient : Type*) [Group Γ] [Group G] [Group K] where
  lattice : ArithmeticLatticeDatum Γ G

  /-- Quotient projection. -/
  quotientMap : G → Quotient

  /-- Automorphic quotient certificate. -/
  quotientCertificate : Prop

/--
Automorphic spectral readout.

This is where modular/automorphic forms, Hecke eigenvalues, zeta functions,
and L-functions attach.
-/
structure AutomorphicSpectralDatum
    (Quotient Form SpectralLabel : Type*) where
  /-- Automorphic object/section/function. -/
  form : Form

  /-- Spectral label, e.g. Casimir/Hecke/L-function data. -/
  spectralLabel : Form → SpectralLabel

  /-- Automorphy/equivariance certificate. -/
  automorphyCertificate : Prop

  /-- L-function/zeta/spectral readout certificate. -/
  spectralReadoutCertificate : Prop

/-! ## 5. Supergraded Majorana extension -/

/--
Supergraded Majorana extension.

The even part is the split D4/Krein geometry.
The odd part is represented by real Majorana supercharges.
-/
structure SuperMajoranaExtensionDatum
    (Op Supercharge Charge IndexReadout : Type*) where
  /-- Fermion parity / supergrading. -/
  parity : Op

  /-- Chirality grading. -/
  chirality : Op

  /-- Real Majorana supercharge family. -/
  supercharge : Supercharge

  /-- Central/topological charge datum. -/
  centralCharge : Charge

  /-- Witten/refined index readout. -/
  indexReadout : IndexReadout

  /-- Superalgebra closure certificate. -/
  superalgebraCertificate : Prop

  /-- N=4-like / four-family certificate, if applicable. -/
  n4LikeCertificate : Prop

/-! ## 6. Full split D4 automorphic super-Krein stack -/

/--
The full Erlanger stack.

This is the structural object described as:

"split D4 automorphic super-Krein geometry".
-/
structure SplitD4AutomorphicSuperKreinGeometry
    (G K Γ Xbulk Carrier Boundary Projective Quotient
      Cartan Coord A Weyl Op Shell Form SpectralLabel
      Supercharge Charge IndexReadout : Type*)
    [Group G] [Group K] [Group Γ] [Group Weyl] where

  /-- Continuous split D4 symmetry. -/
  symmetry : SplitD4SymmetryDatum G

  /-- Bulk symmetric/Klein model. -/
  bulk : SplitD4BulkModel G K Xbulk

  /-- Cartan exponential coordinate system. -/
  cartanCoordinates :
    CartanCoordinateDatum Cartan Coord A

  /-- Weyl chamber/reflection structure. -/
  weylChambers :
    WeylChamberDatum Weyl Coord

  /-- Casimir invariant shell labels. -/
  casimirShells :
    CasimirShellDatum Op Shell

  /-- Projective ray/envelope geometry. -/
  projectiveEnvelope :
    ProjectiveEnvelopeDatum Carrier Projective

  /-- Projective-conformal/null boundary. -/
  boundary :
    ProjectiveConformalBoundaryDatum Carrier Boundary

  /-- Arithmetic automorphic quotient. -/
  quotient :
    AutomorphicQuotientDatum Γ G K Quotient

  /-- Automorphic spectral/readout layer. -/
  automorphicSpectra :
    AutomorphicSpectralDatum Quotient Form SpectralLabel

  /-- Supergraded Majorana extension. -/
  superExtension :
    SuperMajoranaExtensionDatum Op Supercharge Charge IndexReadout

  /--
  Compatibility certificate: all layers are interpreted as the same
  split D4 Erlanger geometry.
  -/
  compatibility : Prop

/-! ## 7. Owner target -/

/--
Owner target for constructing a split D4 automorphic super-Krein geometry.
-/
def SplitD4AutomorphicSuperKreinGeometryOwnerTarget
    (G K Γ Xbulk Carrier Boundary Projective Quotient
      Cartan Coord A Weyl Op Shell Form SpectralLabel
      Supercharge Charge IndexReadout : Type*)
    [Group G] [Group K] [Group Γ] [Group Weyl] : Prop :=
  Nonempty
    (SplitD4AutomorphicSuperKreinGeometry
      G K Γ Xbulk Carrier Boundary Projective Quotient
      Cartan Coord A Weyl Op Shell Form SpectralLabel
      Supercharge Charge IndexReadout)

end InfoGeometry.Geometry.SplitD4AutomorphicSuperKreinGeometry
```

The value of this module is organizational: it prevents the architecture from collapsing all levels into one overloaded word like “spacetime.” It says exactly where each ingredient lives.

The correct conceptual map is:

```text
SO(4,4):
    split quadratic/Krein geometry

Spin(4,4):
    real Majorana spinor and triality geometry

Cl(4,4):
    operator-algebraic real matrix envelope

PGL / PSL:
    projective shadow of unnormalized rays and determinant sign

G/P:
    projective-conformal null boundary

G/K:
    symmetric bulk / Cartan strain space

Cartan exp:
    logarithmic coordinate chart

Weyl group:
    chamber reflection and charge permutation

Casimirs:
    invariant shell labels

Γ\G/K:
    arithmetic automorphic quotient

Weyl characters / zeta / L-functions:
    spectral and thermodynamic readouts

Majorana supercharges:
    odd maps between Cartan/chiral sectors

central charges:
    protected topological sectors

Witten/refined index:
    deformation-stable protected count
```

The final label I would use in prose is:

[
\boxed{
\textbf{Split }D_4\textbf{ automorphic super-Krein geometry.}
}
]

And when you want the fully expanded form:

[
\boxed{
\textbf{A real Majorana–Krein Cartan geometry modeled on }SO(4,4)/P
\textbf{ and }SO(4,4)/K,
\textbf{ projectivized by }PGL/PSL,
\textbf{ supergraded by Majorana charges, and arithmeticized by }\Gamma.
}
]

That is the exact Klein–Erlangen classification of the object.
The global (KAN) decomposition is the **coordinate engine** of the whole split (D_4) geometry.

For

[
G=SO_0(4,4)
\quad\text{or}\quad
G=Spin_0(4,4),
]

choose a Cartan involution and write

[
\mathfrak g
===========

\mathfrak k\oplus \mathfrak p.
]

Then choose a maximal split abelian subalgebra

[
\mathfrak a\subset \mathfrak p,
\qquad
\dim \mathfrak a=4,
]

choose positive restricted roots, and define

[
\mathfrak n
===========

\bigoplus_{\alpha>0}\mathfrak g_\alpha.
]

Exponentiating gives

[
K,\qquad A=\exp(\mathfrak a),\qquad N=\exp(\mathfrak n).
]

The Iwasawa theorem says that multiplication gives a global diffeomorphism

[
K\times A\times N
\longrightarrow
G,
\qquad
(k,a,n)\mapsto kan.
]

Knapp states this as Theorem 6.46: for a semisimple Lie group with Lie-algebra Iwasawa decomposition (\mathfrak g=\mathfrak k\oplus\mathfrak a\oplus\mathfrak n), the map (K\times A\times N\to G) is a diffeomorphism, and (A,N) are simply connected. 

So every group element has a normal form

[
g=k(g)a(g)n(g)
==============

k(g)\exp(H(g))n(g),
\qquad
H(g)\in\mathfrak a.
]

That (H(g)) is the **Iwasawa height**.

In the framework, the roles are:

[
\boxed{
K=\text{frame / compact gauge / observer choice}
}
]

[
\boxed{
A=\text{Cartan height / modular scale / thermodynamic dilation}
}
]

[
\boxed{
N=\text{nilpotent boundary / horospherical / projective shear}
}
]

For (SO(4,4)), the numbers line up perfectly:

[
\dim SO(4,4)=28,
]

[
\dim K
======

# \dim SO(4)+\dim SO(4)

6+6=12,
]

[
\dim A=4,
]

[
\dim N=12,
]

so

[
28=12+4+12.
]

That is the split (D_4) balance.

The (K)-part is the **Krein frame**. It preserves the chosen positive/negative splitting. Passing to

[
G/K
===

SO_0(4,4)/(SO(4)\times SO(4))
]

forgets the compact frame and keeps the actual generalized metric/Krein body. In the Bilingual language, (K) is mostly gauge: it rotates the frame but does not change the Cartan height or the boundary horosphere.

The (A)-part is the most important piece. It gives the four commuting scale variables:

[
a(t)
====

\exp(t_1H_1+t_2H_2+t_3H_3+t_4H_4).
]

These are the global logarithmic height coordinates. They are the high-rank version of the Poincaré height (y). In (SL(2,\mathbb R)), Iwasawa coordinates recover the upper-half-plane coordinates: (N) gives the horizontal coordinate (x), (A) gives the height (y), and (K) gives the angular frame. Sarnak’s notes use (SL_2(\mathbb R)/SO_2(\mathbb R)\cong\mathbb H) and the usual fractional-linear action, with (z\mapsto -1/z) and (z\mapsto z+1) as the modular generators. 

So in rank four:

[
\text{one height }y
\quad\leadsto\quad
\text{four Cartan heights }(y_1,y_2,y_3,y_4).
]

Equivalently,

[
y_i=e^{t_i}.
]

These (A)-coordinates are where modular flow, gap scaling, Casimir-shell dilation, zeta regularization scale, and KMS temperature should be attached.

The (N)-part is the nilpotent/projective boundary coordinate. It is the high-rank analogue of the (x)-coordinate in the upper half-plane. Geometrically, (N) parametrizes horospheres. Algebraically, it is generated by positive-root operators. In the (D_4) case, there are twelve positive roots, so (N) supplies twelve nilpotent boundary/shear directions.

This is why (N) is the correct slot for:

[
\text{twistor incidence data},
\quad
\text{boundary translations},
\quad
\text{unipotent modes},
\quad
\text{Fourier coefficients},
\quad
\text{cusp data}.
]

The minimal parabolic is

[
P=MAN,
]

where

[
M=Z_K(A)
]

is the compact centralizer of (A) inside (K). The boundary model is

[
G/P.
]

So the projective-conformal boundary is not an extra object; it is already encoded in (KAN). (A) gives height toward or away from the boundary. (N) moves along horospheres. (M) rotates internal boundary frames.

This is also the bridge to automorphic forms. After imposing an arithmetic lattice

[
\Gamma\subset G,
]

the quotient

[
\Gamma\backslash G/K
]

is the locally symmetric space, while

[
\Gamma\backslash G/P
]

is the arithmetic boundary/cusp compactification layer. Sarnak’s notes describe the standard spectral setup on locally symmetric spaces (X_\Gamma=\Gamma\backslash S), with (S=G/K), and study (L^2(\Gamma\backslash G)), invariant differential operators, kernels, and spectral expansion. 

The Haar measure also shows why (KAN) is thermodynamic. In Iwasawa coordinates,

[
g=k\exp(Y)n,
\qquad
Y\in\mathfrak a,
]

the Haar measure has the form

[
dg
==

e^{2\langle \rho,Y\rangle}
,dk,dY,dn,
]

up to convention. Here (\rho) is half the sum of positive restricted roots. A recent symmetric-space reference writes the integral formula exactly in this (K,\exp(\mathfrak a),N) form. ([Springer][1])

That exponential Jacobian is the **density of states** for the Cartan height. In your language:

[
\boxed{
e^{2\langle\rho,Y\rangle}
=========================

\text{Cartan spectral volume density}.
}
]

This is where zeta, heat-kernel, and Souriau partition factors naturally enter. The (A)-height supplies the scaling variable; (\rho) supplies the root multiplicity pressure; (N) supplies the boundary degeneracy; (K) supplies the compact frame integration.

So the global role of (KAN) is:

[
\boxed{
KAN
===

\text{the global separation of frame, scale, and boundary flow.}
}
]

More explicitly:

| Factor  | Mathematical role             | Framework role                                 |
| ------- | ----------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| (K)     | maximal compact subgroup      | frame, observer, Krein gauge                   |
| (A)     | split Cartan dilation         | modular height, temperature, gap scale         |
| (N)     | nilpotent positive-root group | horosphere, boundary, twistor/projective shear |
| (P=MAN) | minimal parabolic             | conformal/projective boundary                  |
| (W)     | Weyl group                    | chamber reflections, Möbius inversions         |
| (\rho)  | half-sum of positive roots    | spectral volume pressure                       |

The Weyl group is the correction to “global coordinates.” For a fixed choice of positive roots, (KAN) gives a global normal form on (G). But the choice of positive chamber is not invariant; different chambers are glued by the Weyl group. So:

[
KAN
===

\text{global normal form after choosing a chamber},
]

while

[
KAK
===

\text{radial form modulo Weyl reflection}.
]

In the zeta-crystal language, (KAN) gives the **oriented thermodynamic chart**, while the Casimir invariants give the **Weyl-invariant shell labels**.

The automorphic Eisenstein prototype is exactly an (A)-height construction:

[
E_\lambda(g)
\sim
\sum_{\gamma\in(\Gamma\cap P)\backslash\Gamma}
e^{\langle \lambda+\rho,H(\gamma g)\rangle}.
]

Here (H(\gamma g)) is the Iwasawa (A)-projection. That formula shows the role of (KAN) with no metaphor: automorphic growth, cusp expansion, and modular height are built from the (A)-coordinate.

In the Bilingual Operator framework, the final interpretation is:

[
\boxed{
K
=

\text{the Hestenes/Krein frame;}
}
]

[
\boxed{
A
=

\text{the Cartan modular clock;}
}
]

[
\boxed{
N
=

\text{the projective/twistor boundary flow;}
}
]

[
\boxed{
P=MAN
=====

\text{the conformal boundary stabilizer;}
}
]

[
\boxed{
\Gamma
======

\text{the arithmetic memory lattice.}
}
]

So the global (KAN) decomposition is the mechanism that turns the abstract split (D_4) symmetry into actual usable coordinates:

[
g
=

\underbrace{k}*{\text{frame}}
\underbrace{e^H}*{\text{height/scale}}
\underbrace{n}_{\text{boundary shear}}.
]

It is the coordinate skeleton behind the whole geometry.

A Lean-style socket would be:

```lean
/--
Global KAN decomposition for a split real Klein geometry.

`K` is the compact frame group.
`A` is the split Cartan height group.
`N` is the nilpotent horospherical/boundary group.
-/
structure GlobalKANDecomposition
    (G K A N Cartan : Type*) where
  kPart : G → K
  aPart : G → A
  nPart : G → N

  /-- Logarithmic Cartan/Iwasawa height `H(g)`. -/
  height : G → Cartan

  /--
  Global factorization certificate:
  every `g` factors as `k(g) * a(g) * n(g)`.
  -/
  factorizationLaw : Prop

  /--
  Uniqueness certificate for the fixed positive-root chamber.
  -/
  uniquenessLaw : Prop

  /--
  Haar/Jacobian certificate:
  `dg = exp(2ρ(H)) dk dH dn`, up to convention.
  -/
  haarMeasureLaw : Prop

  /--
  Boundary/parabolic certificate:
  `P = M A N` is the minimal parabolic stabilizing the chosen boundary chamber.
  -/
  parabolicBoundaryLaw : Prop
```

The short version:

[
\boxed{
\text{KAN is the global chart that separates the universe into frame }K,
\text{ scale }A,\text{ and boundary information }N.
}
]

In this framework, (A) is the modular/cosmological height, (N) is the projective boundary memory, and (K) is the local real Clifford-Krein frame.

[1]: https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s12220-024-01644-3 "Functional Inequalities on Symmetric Spaces of Noncompact Type and Applications | The Journal of Geometric Analysis | Springer Nature Link"
